# M1.2 — Query Engine and Native API Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans. Execute task by task, test first. Each task lists the interfaces it must produce and the tests that must exist and pass before it is done. Where this plan gives exact values (names, paths, formats, constants), use them verbatim. The code is not pre-written in this plan (M0.3 Ruling 1).

> **Status: Planned** (2026-09-24).

**Goal:** Ship `operon-query`, the read side of collections and the one facade every gateway uses. It contains:
- the search IR of overview §6.6 with every auxiliary type, and its exact JSON form;
- read snapshots per `ReadConsistency` (strong reads through a linearizable high-watermark read, `AtLeast`, `Eventual`, and `Pinned` reads without a metastore hold);
- the in-memory **tail index** (H3), which makes a write visible to strong reads before the link applies it;
- DataFusion 54.1 operators for BM25 search with global statistics, filter bitmaps, ANN over Lance, exact sparse-vector search with Qdrant's IDF modifier (`SparseExec`, overview A29), fusion, document fetch and tail merge;
- aggregations, highlighting, sorting and paging;
- `CollectionService`;
- a DataFusion catalog with the `vector_search`, `text_search`, `hybrid_search` and `rrf` table functions, with `rerank` reserved for M3 (Spice's names, D56);
- the native REST collection, document, query and SQL endpoints;
- an Arrow Flight SQL service;
- Flight `DoPut` bulk ingest of Arrow record batches into collections and streams (D49);
- scan pinning (D53): a native route that resolves a collection into a pinned scan plan (the manifest version, the Lance dataset URI with its detached version id, the fragments and whether a tail exists), so external readers can read the Lance fragments directly or fall back to Flight.

The plan depends on M1.1 and on M1.2a: every `operon-query` type and the native API hold the metastore as `Arc<dyn MetaStore>` (D47, M1.2a), never the concrete openraft `MetaClient`.

The hot tier (M1.3) and affinity routing plug in through two fixed hook traits. Results with the hot tier off equal results with it on for every exact path, and a property test proves that the tail merge returns exactly what a full rebuild of the durable state returns.

**Architecture:**
- **Reads resolve one `ReadView` per request:** a durable `CollectionSnapshot` at one manifest, plus a `TailSnapshot` relative to that manifest, plus the effective hot tier.
  - `Strong` reads the implicit stream's high watermarks with one linearizable metastore read (`collection_head(Linearizable, …)`), then waits until the tail covers them.
  - `AtLeast` waits for the token's offsets.
  - `Eventual` takes whatever the tail holds.
  - `Pinned` opens a retained manifest and a *range tail* built over exactly `(applied, token]`.
- **The tail is one per collection on the query node.** It follows the implicit stream from the live manifest's `applied` offsets, decodes `DocOp`s, and folds them per key over the durable state. It keeps a RAM Tantivy index with the splits' mapping and analyzers, and flat vectors. A tail entry overrides the durable row of its key iff the entry's offset is at or above the manifest's `applied` offset for its partition (Ruling 1). The durable rows it supersedes form the snapshot's *shadow* set, kept exact across manifest commits through the commits' PK deltas.
- **Every operator works in one row-id space.** Durable rows use their Lance stable row ids; tail docs use ids at or above `TAIL_ROWID_BASE = 2^63` (Ruling 7).
  - Deleted split docs and shadowed rows are masked inside every operator, before any top-k cut.
  - `TailMergeExec` merges durable and tail streams in primary-key order, for scans, scroll and SQL.
- **Text:** `TantivySearchExec` searches every split plus the tail with block-max WAND. It uses *global, live-only* BM25 statistics through Tantivy's `Bm25StatisticsProvider` (R6, Ruling 2). `FilterBitmapExec` turns a `Query` into a row-id set.
- **Vectors:** `AnnExec` runs Lance `nearest` with allow or block masks, brute force for exact and small candidate sets, and tail brute force. It rescores every returned score with Operon's own deterministic kernel (R12, Ruling 3).
- **Sparse vectors:** `SparseExec` finds candidates through the `_sparse.<name>` postings of every split and the tail, masks deleted, shadowed and filtered docs, and scores each candidate exactly from its `_sparse_w.<name>` weights, with live-only IDF statistics (Ruling 21, overview R22). It never consults a `HotAnn`; pinned split files serve it like text.
- **Fusion and assembly:** `FusionExec` implements RRF, DBSF and weighted sums exactly as §6.6 defines them (DBSF with Qdrant's unclamped formula, Ruling 4). `DocFetchExec` fetches the final hits with one coalesced Lance take.
- **`CollectionService`** implements overview §6.7:
  - writes through M1.1's `CollectionWriter`, with existence reporting, atomic validation and dynamic mapping;
  - schema updates, aliases, versions and pins;
  - forwarding of reads to their owner through the `Placement`/`RemoteReads` hooks;
  - a per-request hot switch carried by a task-local scope (Ruling 11).
- **SQL and REST:** a DataFusion catalog maps namespace → `collections` schema → `CollectionProvider`. The native REST API and an Arrow Flight SQL server run in the `operon` binary.
- **Scan pinning:** `CollectionService::scan_plan` resolves `current`, a manifest version or a consistency token into a `ScanPlan`: the Lance version the manifest names (Lance's own deletion files already exclude every deleted and superseded row, Ruling 22), its fragments and readable columns, the applied offsets against the requested ones (`tail`), a `pin` for fallback reads of the same state, and the retention deadline. It reads only the metastore, manifests and the Lance manifest, so any node serves it.
- **Bulk ingest:** Flight `DoPut` (with a path descriptor, or with Flight SQL's `CommandStatementIngest`, which ADBC's `adbc_ingest` sends) maps each Arrow record batch either to `DocOp::Upsert`s written through `CollectionService::write` (the native bulk write path) or to stream records appended through the native produce path. SQL stays read-only.

## Global Constraints

Same as the M1 overview §8, plus:
- **One ordering everywhere:** the effective sort keys, then canonical PK bytes ascending (R10). Every path gives equal hits in the same order: splits and tail, one split or many, hot on or off, local or forwarded. No result may depend on `HashMap` iteration order, task completion order or split open order. Intermediate results are merged in manifest split order, then the tail.
- **The tail is never a source of truth.** Dropping every tail at any moment changes only latency. Nothing the tail holds is persisted.
- **Scores are exact** wherever this plan says so. Every vector score returned is computed by `vector::score` (Task 6). No returned score comes from Lance's distance column or from a `HotAnn`.
- **Serde forms are wire formats.** Every IR and service type has exactly the JSON form of Task 1. `SearchRequest` → JSON → `SearchRequest` is the identity after `normalized()`, because M1.3 forwards reads with it and the SDKs (M1.6) speak it. JSON never carries a non-finite number: validation rejects them on input, and no path produces one on output.
- **The metastore is reached only through `Arc<dyn MetaStore>`** (M1.2a, D47). No type in `operon-query` or in the native API names the openraft `MetaClient`, and no code here reads `MetaState` through a closure. The trait methods used are: `create_namespace`, `create_collection`, `drop_collection`, `update_collection_schema` and `update_aliases` (M1.1's signatures, kept on the trait); `namespace_by_name`, `namespaces`, `resolve_collection`, `collection`, `collections`, `aliases`, `pointer` (through M1.1's `live_manifest`); `collection_head(consistency, cid)`, which returns the collection, its pointer, the implicit stream's log start offsets and high watermarks and the metastore clock from one state; `clock_ms`, `now_ms`; `watch_changes() -> MetaChanges`; `stream_by_name` (the produce path). `LogReader` pages the offset index with `partition_index` (M1.2a). `collection` and `collection_head` return a collection whatever its namespace: every caller compares `collection.namespace` with the namespace it resolved, and a mismatch is `NotFound { kind: "collection" }` (row 0.73). `MetaChanges::changed` returning `Err(MetaStopped)` means the metastore stopped: a loop that waits on it stops waiting on it (row 0.79). `operon-query` depends on `operon-common` for the metastore types, never on `operon-meta` outside `[dev-dependencies]` (row 0.86).
- `CollectionService` is the only code in `operon-query` that calls `MetaStore` write operations. Gateways call nothing below it (overview §8).
- **SQL is read-only on every served surface** (REST `…/sql`, Flight SQL): `SQLOptions::new().with_allow_ddl(false).with_allow_dml(false).with_allow_statements(false)`. `sql_context` never enables URL tables. Flight `DoPut` (Task 13) writes through `CollectionService::write` and the native produce path, never through SQL.
- Commit areas used here: `query`, `collection`, `log`, `operon`, `ci`, `docs`.

## Rulings made while writing this plan

| # | Ruling | Why | Cost if wrong |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Tail overlay rule.** A tail entry for key *k* in partition *p* (the latest op on *k* the tail has folded, at offset *o*) overrides the durable state iff `o >= manifest.applied[p]`. Entries below `applied` are *covered*: the manifest already reflects them, so they are ignored and later dropped. One live tail therefore serves every manifest at or after its base. Reads that need an upper bound below the tail's head (`Pinned`) or that the live tail cannot serve (overflow) use a *range tail*: a tail built from the log over exactly `(applied, upper]` | Latest-by-key state cannot answer "state at offset *T*" for a *T* below its head. The `applied` comparison makes the overlay correct for any manifest the tail has adopted, with no rebuild per commit | A range tail costs one log read of the range per distinct `(manifest, upper)` pair, cached (Task 4) |
| 2 | **BM25 statistics are global and live-only** (R6). Over all splits of the manifest plus the tail:<ul><li>`total_num_docs` = live, unshadowed durable rows + live tail docs;</li><li>`doc_freq(term)` = postings minus deleted and shadowed docs;</li><li>`total_num_tokens(field)` = Σ over the same docs of the *midpoint* of each doc's quantized fieldnorm bucket (Task 5).</li></ul> | Lucene counts deleted docs until a merge. That would make scores depend on where a document's history lives (splits with delete bitmaps, or the tail), and the tail-merge property test could never pass. Fieldnorms are the only per-doc length Tantivy keeps, and the bucket midpoint is an unbiased estimate of the exact token count Lucene averages | `avgdl` differs from Lucene's by the quantization error of long documents (bucket width ≤ 1/8 of the length). M1.7's BEIR gate measures the effect |
| 3 | **Operon's own kernel scores every vector result** (`vector::score`: f64 accumulation in index order, then rounded to f32). Exact search (`AnnParams.exact`, a metric override, or a candidate set under the brute-force threshold) never uses an index, Lance's distance kernels or a `HotAnn`. It is Operon's brute force over the live rows | R12 needs bit-identical exact results with the hot tier on and off, and a SIMD kernel's summation order differs between libraries. R12 also requires every approximate score to be exact | One extra vector read for Lance-index hits (the vector column is projected in the same scan, not taken again) |
| 4 | **DBSF is Qdrant's `distr_norm` exactly:** f32 Welford mean and sample variance, `(s − (μ − 3σ)) / 6σ`, **not clamped**. A list of one hit scores 0.5, and so does a list with σ = 0 (`qdrant-edge-0.8.0/src/segment/common/score_fusion.rs:113-162`). The overview's first draft said "clamped to [0, 1]"; the overview adopted this ruling as A20 | M1.4's contract test S2 compares against the Qdrant formula. Qdrant does not clamp, and scores outside 3σ are ordinary | Callers that assumed [0, 1] see values slightly outside it |
| 5 | **Fusion arithmetic is f32**, summed in retriever order:<ul><li>RRF `Σ 1/(k + rank)`, with ranks 1-based within each list;</li><li>WeightedSum `Σ wᵢ·sᵢ`;</li><li>DBSF as in Ruling 4.</li></ul>A doc is identified across lists by its row id | Qdrant computes RRF in f32 (`reciprocal_rank_fusion.rs:32-39`), so M1.4's mapping reproduces it bit for bit: Qdrant's `1/(pos + k_q)` with 0-based `pos` is `1/(rank + (k_q − 1))`, so Qdrant's `k_q` is sent as `Rrf { k: k_q − 1 }` (overview §6.6; Qdrant's default 2 becomes 1) | — |
| 6 | **Two ranking modes.** *Score mode* (`sort` empty, or its first key is `Score`): candidates are the retrievers' top-k lists, fused. *Field mode* (the first key is `Field` or `Pk`): at most one retriever, which must be `Text`. The candidates are **all** docs matching `Text ∧ filter` (the retriever's `k` is ignored), ordered by the sort keys, as ES sorts every match. Aggregation domain: the matches of `Text ∧ filter` when the only retriever is `Text`; the fused candidate set when any retriever is not `Text`; the filter's matches when there is no retriever | ES semantics for `query` + `sort` and for aggregations with `knn` | A vector retriever combined with a field sort is refused (`InvalidArgument`) |
| 7 | **Tail row ids are `TAIL_ROWID_BASE (1 << 63) + seq`.** `seq` is a per-tail counter that is never reused within a process. Lance stable row ids are below 2^63 | One `RoaringTreemap` space for masks, bitmaps, fusion and fetch | A Lance dataset with 2^63 rows |
| 8 | **Table functions take positional arguments.** DataFusion 54 refuses named arguments of a table function in `FROM` (`datafusion-sql-54.1.0/src/relation/mod.rs:156-170` accepts only `FunctionArg::Unnamed`). So §05 §4's `vector => (…)` form becomes, for example, `hybrid_search('memories', 'embedding', [0.12, …], 'body', 'refund policy', 100)` (Task 10) | The planner path is not ours to change | §05 §4 is amended in Task 16 |
| 9 | **JSON forms:**<ul><li>integers are `I64` when they fit, else `U64`;</li><li>a UUID sort value or primary key is `{"uuid": "8-4-4-4-12"}`;</li><li>a date field value is `{"date": "<RFC 3339, µs, Z>"}`;</li><li>a bare RFC 3339 string is `Str` and is coerced by the field's kind;</li><li>the PK tie-break sort value is the key's own JSON (`SortValue::as_pk`).</li></ul>No `Pk(PrimaryKey)` sort value exists | Every value round-trips exactly through JSON (M1.3 forwarding, SDKs), and an SDK sees plain scalars. M1.5 assumed `SortValue::Pk` and M1.6 "any JSON scalar"; this satisfies M1.6 and gives M1.5 `as_pk()` | — |
| 10 | **Service write types:**<ul><li>`OpResult` is the enum `{Created, Updated, Deleted, NotFound, Noop, Accepted, Rejected(ServiceError)}` (M1.5's shape, extended), with `existed()` for M1.4;</li><li>`WriteOptions` is `{report_existence, atomic}` (`Default`);</li><li>`WriteResult` carries `positions` (partition, `seq_no`) per op.</li></ul>A patch of a missing key without `upsert` is `NotFound`, not `Noop`: `Noop` means "present and unchanged", which only a patch can be (an upsert always writes a new version, M1.1 P32; row 0.53) | M1.4 and M1.5 assumed incompatible shapes (a struct with `existed` against an enum). The enum carries more, and `existed()` recovers M1.4's view. ES answers `document_missing_exception` for an `_update` of a missing id, which is exactly `NotFound` | M1.4 and M1.5 adapt in their Task 0 |
| 11 | **The hot switch is a task-local scope** (`hot::scope`, set by `HotLayer` from the `Operon-Hot` header or `operon-hot` gRPC metadata). `CollectionService` reads it once at method entry and uses `NoHotTier` when it is off. The structures used are recorded in the scope and in `SearchResponse.hot_used`, and `HotLayer` writes `Operon-Hot-Used` | The §6.7 method signatures stay verbatim for every gateway, and the switch reaches forwarded reads through the `RemoteReads` implementation | Work spawned without the scope sees the server default; the service captures the scope before spawning (Task 9) |
| 12 | `SourceFilter` is `{All, None, Paths { include, exclude }}` (M1.4's name, M1.5's shape). Path patterns are ES `_source` filters (Task 7) | One type serves both gateways; M1.4 uses only `All` and `None` | — |
| 13 | `CollectionInfo.created_at_ms` comes from the annotation `operon.created_at_ms`, which `create_collection` writes. Retry-safety compares schemas ignoring that annotation | The M1.1 catalog record has no creation time, and adding one to the Raft state is not worth a snapshot format bump | Collections created below the service report 0 |
| 14 | **Pins hold nothing** (amendment A6). `pin()` returns `PinnedRead { collection, name, manifest_version, token }`, taken with one linearizable read. A pinned read of a manifest that is no longer retained, or of a token for another stream, fails with `NotFound { kind: "pin" }`. Manifest version 0 (before the first commit) is served by a range tail from offset 0 while the log still starts at 0 | M1.5's `_delete_by_query` needs a stable view (point in time is dropped with D48; M1.4's snapshots only list `versions`), and A6 replaced the metastore hold | A pinned read fails once its manifest has `keep_manifests` newer manifests and was superseded more than `time_travel_retention` ago (retention runs from supersession, overview A21) |
| 15 | **Scroll walks the `_pk` term dictionaries** of the splits in a k-way merge with the tail's key order, through a per-split PK dictionary cache. A filter whose durable matches are ≤ `scroll_sort_threshold` (100 000) instead sorts those matches' keys | Pages cost O(page × splits × log) instead of a full scan per page. The term dictionary of the raw `_pk` field is already in canonical PK order | Cached `_pk` dictionaries use memory, bounded by weight |
| 16 | **Native REST writes are atomic:** the service validates every op first and writes nothing if any op fails (`WriteOptions.atomic`). Gateways choose per-item results (`atomic: false`) | An SDK caller expects one request to succeed or fail as a whole; ES `_bulk` needs per-item errors | Validation runs twice (service and writer) |
| 17 | **HTTP error fixes** (overview §9, as-built row):<ul><li>`VersionMismatch`, `Fenced`, `LeaseHeld`, `LeaseLost` and `SchemaVersionMismatch` → 409 `conflict`;</li><li>`StaleObject`, `StaleCommit` and `IndexMismatch` → 503 `unavailable` with `Retry-After: 1`;</li><li>every 503 carries `Retry-After: 1`.</li></ul>A record produced to an implicit stream (`_collection.*`) must have a key that decodes as a `PrimaryKey` with `partition_of(pk) == partition`, else 400 | A conflict is the caller's to resolve and must not look like a server bug; the other three succeed on retry. A misrouted record would break per-key order | M1.1 Task 3 item 13 already maps `SchemaVersionMismatch` to 409 `conflict`; this plan changes the other four and the 503s |
| 18 | `CollectionService` owns the dynamic-mapping step (R17) for `DynamicMapping::Map`: before appending, it proposes the missing fields with M1.1's `propose_dynamic_fields` and CASes the schema, retrying `SchemaVersionMismatch` up to 5 times | Every surface gets ES dynamic mapping without duplicating it; a gateway that maps first finds nothing left to add | — |
| 19 | **The live tail indexes with the *current* schema.** A schema version change rebuilds the tail's Tantivy index. The link writes each split with the schema current at its commit, so tail and splits index the same doc with the same schema once it is applied | A4 says old *splits* are not backfilled; docs still in the tail are re-indexed when their split is written anyway | A tail rebuild per schema change (rare) |
| 20 | **Collections default to 4 partitions** (`ServiceConfig.default_partitions`) | Four parallel append streams, and one link task per collection (R9) | — |
| 21 | **Sparse search is exhaustive and exact over live docs** (overview A29, R22). Candidates = the union of the query indices' postings in `_sparse.<name>`, minus deleted and shadowed docs, intersected with the filter's rows. Every candidate is scored: `Σ q'ᵢ · wᵢ` over shared indices in ascending index order, f32 accumulation (Qdrant's `score_vectors`), `q'ᵢ = qᵢ · idfᵢ` for `Idf` fields, `idfᵢ = ln((N − dfᵢ + 0.5)/(dfᵢ + 0.5) + 1)` computed in f32 from u64 counts converted to f32. `N` = live postings of `SPARSE_PRESENT`, `dfᵢ` = live postings of term *i*, both over the manifest's splits and the tail and, with `idf_corpus`, over the corpus rows only; per-split counts without a corpus are cached in `StatsCache` under `(split ulid, delete-bitmap path, field, term)`, shadowed docs are subtracted per request (as Ruling 2 does for BM25) | Identical results hot or cold and across split layouts (R12, Task 15) require one deterministic formula over one live-doc set; Qdrant's server counts deleted-but-unoptimized points in its global statistics (`qc:qdrant_client/local/local_collection.py:291-323` documents it) while its local mode and its corpus scope count live points, which is what this reproduces | No pruning: a query index present in most documents makes most documents candidates (overview R22 cost) |
| 22 | **A scan plan needs no delete bitmap: Lance's own deletion files are authoritative for Lance readers** (D53). M1.1's commit deletes every removed and every superseded row in Lance itself (M1.1 Task 10 rule 3.6: `delete_rows(old row ids)` writes Lance deletion files and commits `Operation::Update`; an upsert of an existing key deletes the old row), so the Lance version a manifest names holds exactly that manifest's live rows (`live_doc_count`). Every reader that goes through Lance (the `lance` crate, pylance, `lance-ray`, Daft) applies deletion files itself. The plan therefore lists each fragment's deletion file (path, kind, count) for readers that read data files without Lance, and leaves out the split delete bitmaps, which address Tantivy doc ids and mirror the same deletions. What Lance cannot hold is the tail: records after the manifest's `applied` offsets. The plan reports them (`tail`, `tail_records`, per-partition `applied` against `target`) and carries a `pin` (`{manifest_version, token}`) that reads exactly the requested state, tail included, through Flight SQL or the native API (Task 14 rule 6). A reader that must not miss the tail uses the pin or asks with `at: {"token": …}`, which waits until a manifest covers it | The deletions are already materialised where external readers look, so a second deletion structure would only duplicate them and could disagree. The tail is the one state a Lance reader cannot see, so the plan says so instead of hiding it | If a later plan (M1.3 compaction, Phase B) ever leaves deleted rows in a Lance version without deletion files, scan plans become wrong for direct readers; `the_lance_version_holds_exactly_the_live_documents` (Task 14) fails first |
| 23 | **SQL search functions use Spice's names and argument order** (D56). `vector_search(collection, query_vector [, field [, k [, filter_json [, exact]]]])` and `text_search(collection, text [, field [, k [, filter_json]]])` follow Spice's `(table, query, [column], [limit])`; `field` may be omitted or `NULL` when the collection has exactly one dense vector (one text field), and `k` defaults to 1 000 (Spice's `text_search` default). `rrf(retriever_1, retriever_2 [, …] [, k [, limit]])` fuses nested `vector_search`/`text_search` calls of one collection with `Fusion::Rrf`, `k` 60 by default (Spice's `k => 60.0`; a float must be a whole number). `hybrid_search` stays as Operon's two-field shorthand. `rerank` is registered only to refuse: `rerank arrives in M3 (D56); use rrf or hybrid_search`. Arguments stay positional (Ruling 8), so Spice's named arguments (`join_key =>`, `k =>`, `rank_weight =>`, `limit =>`) are not accepted, Operon takes a query vector where Spice embeds text (Operon hosts no models), and every function outputs `_score` (Spice: `score`, `fused_score`), because collection field names cannot start with `_` and so cannot collide with it | A Spice user's `FROM rrf(vector_search(t, …), text_search(t, …))` keeps its shape. Positional arguments are DataFusion 54's limit (Ruling 8) | If DataFusion does not fold the nested calls (Task 10 rule 5), `rrf` takes retriever JSON strings instead; the names stay |
| 24 | **A token scan waits for durability, not for the tail.** `at: {"token": t}` waits up to `consistency_wait` (10 s, as strong reads, Task 4) for a live manifest whose `applied` offsets cover `t`, then plans that manifest; past the deadline it is `Timeout`, as a strong read that cannot catch up. `current` does not wait: it plans the live manifest and reports the tail. A manifest version is planned as it is (its target is its own `applied`, so `tail` is false) | An external reader cannot see the tail, so waiting for the tail (what a strong read does) would not help it; waiting for the link to apply the token does. D52's tags use the same rule | A lagging link makes token scans time out; the caller retries or plans `current` and uses the pin |

|---|---|---|
| M0 known limitations | Fetches read a whole WAL chunk even for a small range | 3 (`LogReader` learns each chunk's batch boundaries and reads from the batch that holds the offset; the tail fetches sequentially) |
| As built | The HTTP API maps `VersionMismatch`, `Fenced` and `StaleObject` to 500 | 11 (Ruling 17) |
| As built | Produce has no key partitioner | 9 (collection writes go through `CollectionWriter`, which routes by `partition_of`), 11 (produce onto an implicit stream is checked against `partition_of`) |

## Review Focus

1. **A write that lands while a manifest commit lands.** Cases: one key's ops split across the commit; a patch whose durable base the commit just replaced; a tail delete of a key the commit re-created. A read must never see two versions of a key, a resurrected delete or a lost patch. Tests: Task 3 (`the_tail_drops_covered_entries_when_a_manifest_lands`, `shadowed_rows_follow_pk_deltas`, `a_patch_across_a_manifest_boundary_folds_once`, `in_flight_snapshots_survive_a_manifest_adoption`), Task 15 (`tail_merge_equals_full_rebuild`, `strong_reads_during_link_apply_never_miss_or_duplicate`).
2. **BM25 statistics with superseded documents and old-schema splits.** A document updated three times must count once. A split written before a field existed must add nothing for that field. The tail's docs must count exactly as they will once in a split. Tests: Task 5 (`global_statistics_equal_a_single_split`, `superseded_versions_do_not_count_in_statistics`, `a_split_without_the_field_contributes_no_tokens`, `bm25_matches_a_reference_implementation`), Task 15.
3. **Ties at every boundary.** Cases: equal scores at the k-th place inside a WAND-pruned split; mixed `U64`/`Uuid`/`Str` keys; equal fused scores; `search_after` with equal sort values; hot on and off. Tests: Task 5 (`ties_at_the_kth_score_are_kept_and_ordered_by_pk`, `top_k_equals_exhaustive_scoring`), Task 7 (`equal_scores_order_by_pk_on_every_path`, `search_after_prefix_skips_equal_values`), Task 15 (`results_are_identical_across_placements_and_hot_modes`).
4. **Pinned reads and tokens that do not fit the snapshot.** Cases: a token of another collection; a manifest leaving retention while a pinned scroll pages; a pin before the first commit; a pinned read after a drop and re-create of the name. Tests: Task 4 (`a_pinned_token_of_another_collection_is_not_found`, `a_pin_before_the_first_commit_reads_from_offset_zero`, `a_pinned_read_after_drop_and_recreate_is_not_found`), Task 15 (`pinned_reads_are_stable_under_writes_commits_and_gc`).
5. **Filtered vector search at the selectivity extremes.** Cases: a filter that matches nothing; one that matches only tail docs; one that matches only shadowed rows; one that matches everything; exact against approximate scores; a wrong dimension; a zero vector. Tests: Task 6 (`a_filter_matching_nothing_returns_nothing`, `a_filter_matching_only_tail_docs_uses_the_tail`, `shadowed_rows_are_never_returned`, `a_broad_filter_postfilters_and_retries`, `ann_scores_are_exact`, `zero_vector_cosine_scores_zero`).
6. **A bulk put that fails part way.** Cases: a bad row in the third batch; a schema error in the first message; a message over the size limit; a misrouted key on an implicit stream. Acknowledged chunks must stay written and readable, the failing chunk must write nothing, and the error must name the batch, the row and the rows already written. Tests: Task 13 (`a_bad_batch_fails_after_earlier_batches_are_acknowledged`, `a_bad_schema_writes_nothing`, `a_message_over_the_limit_is_refused`, `do_put_onto_an_implicit_stream_checks_partitions`).
7. **A scan plan an external reader cannot trust.** Cases: a key upserted twice and a deleted key (the Lance version must hold only the newest row and no deleted one); unapplied writes (the plan must say `tail` and its pin must read them); a manifest leaving retention after it was planned; a token the link has not applied yet; a vector added after the first commit (its column absent from older Lance versions). Tests: Task 14 (`the_lance_version_holds_exactly_the_live_documents`, `unapplied_writes_set_the_tail_flag`, `the_pin_reads_the_tail_through_flight_sql`, `expiry_follows_retention`, `a_token_plan_waits_for_the_link`, `columns_follow_the_lance_version`).

## File structure

```
Cargo.toml                                   # + tower, http; operon-query member and workspace dep
.github/workflows/ci.yml                     # nightly tail-merge property step (Task 15)
crates/operon-query/ (new)
  Cargo.toml
  src/lib.rs
  src/ir.rs  src/types.rs  src/error.rs  src/validate.rs
  src/json/{mod.rs,pk.rs,token.rs,values.rs,schema.rs,hybrid.rs}
  src/hot.rs  src/placement.rs
  src/text/{mod.rs,fields.rs,coerce.rs,compile.rs,splits.rs,stats.rs,norms.rs,highlight.rs,pkdict.rs}
  src/tail/{mod.rs,index.rs,apply.rs,follower.rs,snapshot.rs,range.rs,registry.rs}
  src/read.rs  src/vector.rs  src/sparse.rs
  src/exec/{mod.rs,schema.rs,mask.rs,tantivy_search.rs,filter_bitmap.rs,ann.rs,sparse.rs,fusion.rs,doc_fetch.rs,
            tail_merge.rs,order.rs,planner.rs,groups.rs,aggs.rs,scroll.rs,get.rs,project.rs}
  src/service.rs  src/write.rs  src/catalog_cache.rs
  src/sql/{mod.rs,catalog.rs,provider.rs,udtf.rs,exprs.rs,json_rows.rs}
  src/flight.rs  src/flight_ingest.rs  src/scan.rs
  tests/{ir_json.rs,compile.rs,tail.rs,reads.rs,text.rs,ann.rs,sparse.rs,search.rs,aggs.rs,service.rs,sql.rs,flight.rs,flight_ingest.rs,scan.rs,
         tail_vs_rebuild.rs,strong_reads.rs,pinned.rs,determinism.rs,hot_hooks.rs,common/mod.rs}
  tests/data/{hybrid_request.json,hybrid_body.json}
crates/operon-log/src/reader.rs              # chunk batch boundaries (Task 3)   tests/fetch_ranges.rs (new)
crates/operon/
  Cargo.toml                                 # feature flight (default); deps operon-query, operon-collection, tower
  src/api.rs → src/api/mod.rs (moved) + src/api/{collections.rs,query.rs,sql.rs,errors.rs,streams.rs}
  src/server.rs  src/main.rs
  tests/{native_collections.rs,native_query.rs,native_sql.rs,flight_sql.rs,flight_ingest.rs,native_scan.rs,http.rs}
docs/design/{04-hot-tier.md,05-query-engine.md,06-search-and-vector.md,09-links-and-workers.md,13-decision-log.md,17-ai-data-ecosystem.md}
CHANGELOG.md  README.md  docs/plans/README.md
```

Test layout (row 0.89): every crate has one integration-test binary, `tests/it/main.rs`, which declares one module per `tests/it/<name>.rs` and `mod common;` for `tests/it/common/mod.rs`. A test file this plan names `tests/<name>.rs` is `tests/it/<name>.rs`, `tests/common/mod.rs` is `tests/it/common/mod.rs`, and data files stay in `tests/data/` (read through `env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")`). A named test run is `cargo test -p <crate> --test it <filter>`.

**Semantics (exact steps):**
1. Read the merged `operon-common`, `operon-meta` (collection catalog), `operon-collection`, `operon-text`, `operon-quickwit` and `crates/operon/src/server.rs`.
2. For every item above, add one row to "Rulings made during execution": `item | as built (file:line) | difference | resolution`. Check these in particular:
   - the field names of the vendored `WarmupInfo`, and the constructor of `BuildTantivyAstContext`;
   - the signature of the vendored `merge_intermediate_aggregation_result`: Task 8 uses it when it takes in-memory `IntermediateAggregationResults`, else `IntermediateAggregationResults::merge_fruits`;
   - that `CollectionSnapshot::get_by_pk` returns `row_id`;
   - whether `tantivy_layout` places the Json companions directly after their main field, which Task 2's name-based resolution does not depend on.
3. **M1.2a's as-built `MetaStore` trait.** Read `operon_common::meta` (`store.rs`, `views.rs`), `impl MetaStore for MetaClient` and every call site M1.2a moved in `operon-collection`, `operon-log` and `crates/operon`. Add one row per mapping below, `plan call | trait method (file:line) | difference | resolution`:
   - `create_namespace`, `create_collection`, `drop_collection`, `update_collection_schema`, `update_aliases`: the same signatures on the trait;
   - a namespace by name → `namespace_by_name`; a collection by name or alias → `resolve_collection`; by id → `collection`; a namespace's collections → `collections(Local, Some(ns))`; aliases → `aliases`;
   - every read that needs the collection, its pointer and the implicit stream's bounds together (the tail's follow step, Task 4's `Strong` and `pin` reads, `get_collection`) → `collection_head(consistency, cid)`, one state (`CollectionHead { collection, pointer, log_start_offsets, high_watermarks, clock_ms }`);
   - the metastore clock → `clock_ms(Linearizable)`; the proposer clock → `now_ms()`;
   - `watch_applied` → `watch_changes() -> MetaChanges` (armed when created);
   - `LogReader`'s offset-index reads → `partition_index` (M1.2a moved them; Task 3's chunk-boundary cache sits on top);
   - the produce path's stream by name → `stream_by_name` (Task 13 rule 7).

   Rows 0.12, 0.13, 0.56 and 0.57 below were checked against M1.1's closure-based `MetaClient`; re-check them against the trait.
4. Resolution rules:
   - A rename or a module move: use the as-built name everywhere in this plan.
   - A missing capability that this plan adds itself (the `LogReader` chunk-boundary cache, Task 3; the 409/503 error mapping, Task 11): unchanged. `CollectionSnapshot::at` is M1.1's (its Task 9); if it is missing, stop as below.
   - A metastore call with no `MetaStore` operation: stop and raise it with the M1.2a owner before Task 1. This plan never adds a trait operation of its own, and never downcasts `Arc<dyn MetaStore>` to `MetaClient`.
   - Any other missing capability: stop and raise it with the M1.1 owner before Task 1.
5. **Q20: can external readers open a detached Lance version by id?** (D53; decision log Q20.) Run once by hand; nothing is committed:
   1. With the merged M1.1 code, write a scratch test (under `target/`, deleted afterwards; `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` inside the repository's `target/`) that makes a `LanceEnv` over a `file://` store in a temporary directory, creates version 1, commits two detached versions with `LanceCommitter` (an append of 3 rows, then a delete of 1 of them) and prints the dataset path, the second detached id and its `_versions/d<id>.manifest` path.
   2. Rust: `DatasetBuilder::from_uri("file://<dir>/…/lance").with_version(<id>).load()` with Lance's default session and object store registry (no Operon provider) reads exactly the 2 live rows.
   3. Python: with pylance of the release that matches the workspace's `lance` crate (12.0.x, verify the pairing on PyPI), `uv run --with pylance==<v> python -c "import lance; print(lance.dataset('<path>', version=<id>).count_rows())"` prints 2. The id has bit 63 set, so it exceeds `i64::MAX`: check that the binding accepts it.
   4. If step 3 fails: try `lance.LanceDataset('<path>', serialized_manifest=<bytes of the manifest object>)` (verify the parameter name), which opens a dataset from its manifest bytes. If that works too, record it as the fallback opener.
   5. Record the outcome as one row after the M1.2a rows: `Q20 | pylance <version> | by id: yes/no; by manifest bytes: yes/no | resolution`. Resolution: by id works → nothing changes; only the manifest bytes work → Task 14 is unchanged (the plan already carries `lance.manifest_path`), and the M2 readers (D54) open through it; neither works → Task 14 is unchanged, the M2 readers read through the plan's `pin` over Flight SQL until a custom opener exists, and the owner is told. Task 16 writes the answer into the decision log's Q20.
6. Commit only this plan.

**Tests:** none (no code). Done when every Consumes item and every metastore call of step 3 has a row, and step 5 has its row.

**Commit:** `docs: reconcile the M1.2 plan with the as-built M1.1 and M1.2a code`.

### Task 1: `operon-query` crate — the search IR, service types, errors, the JSON wire form and the hook traits

**Files:** `Cargo.toml` (member, `[workspace.dependencies] operon-query`, `tower`, `http`), `crates/operon-query/{Cargo.toml,src/lib.rs,src/ir.rs,src/types.rs,src/error.rs,src/validate.rs,src/json/{mod.rs,pk.rs,token.rs,values.rs,schema.rs,hybrid.rs},src/hot.rs,src/placement.rs}`, `crates/operon-query/tests/{it/main.rs,it/ir_json.rs,data/hybrid_request.json,data/hybrid_body.json}`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (the metastore boundary step also covers `operon-query`, row 0.87).

**Produces:**

```rust
// ir.rs — every type: Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize; enums #[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")];
// fields marked (d) are #[serde(default)] with the default in the comment. PrimaryKey fields use `json::pk`.
pub struct SearchRequest {
    pub collection: String,
    pub consistency: ReadConsistency,                  // (d) Strong
    pub retrievers: Vec<Retriever>,                    // (d) []
    pub fusion: Option<Fusion>,                        // (d) None
    pub filter: Option<Query>,                         // (d) None
    pub sort: Vec<SortKey>,                            // (d) []
    pub offset: usize,                                 // (d) 0
    pub limit: usize,                                  // (d) 10
    pub search_after: Option<Vec<SortValue>>,          // (d) None
    pub score_threshold: Option<f32>,                  // (d) None
    pub select: Projection,                            // (d) Projection::default()
    pub aggregations: Option<serde_json::Value>,       // (d) None
    pub highlight: Option<Highlight>,                  // (d) None
    pub group_by: Option<GroupBy>,                     // (d) None
    pub track_total_hits: TrackTotalHits,              // (d) None
}
impl SearchRequest { pub fn new(collection: impl Into<String>) -> Self; pub fn normalized(self) -> Self; }  // normalized: every SortValue/FieldValue normalized
pub enum ReadConsistency { #[default] Strong, Eventual, AtLeast(ConsistencyToken), Pinned { manifest_version: u64, token: ConsistencyToken } }
pub enum Retriever {
    Vector { field: String, query: Vec<f32>, k: usize, params: AnnParams /* (d) */, filter: Option<Query> /* (d) */ },
    Text { query: Query, k: usize },
    Fused { inputs: Vec<Retriever>, fusion: Fusion, k: usize },
    Rescore { input: Box<Retriever>, field: String, query: Vec<f32>, k: usize },
    Sparse { field: String, query: SparseVector, k: usize, filter: Option<Query> /* (d) */, params: SparseParams /* (d) */ },   // overview A29
}
#[derive(Default)] pub struct SparseParams { pub idf_corpus: Option<Query> /* (d) */ }
pub use operon_collection::SparseVector;                                  // JSON {"indices": [u32], "values": [f32]}, validated on input
#[derive(Default)] pub struct AnnParams { pub exact: bool, pub nprobes: Option<u32>, pub refine_factor: Option<u32>, pub ef: Option<u32>,
                                          pub oversampling: Option<f32>, pub distance: Option<Distance> }   // every field (d); distance via json::schema::distance (row 0.2)
pub enum Fusion { Rrf { k: u32 /* (d) 60 */ }, Dbsf, WeightedSum { weights: Vec<f32> } }
pub enum Query {
    MatchAll, MatchNone,
    Match { field: String, text: String, operator: BoolOperator /* (d) Or */, minimum_should_match: Option<String> /* (d) */,
            fuzziness: Option<Fuzziness> /* (d) */, analyzer: Option<String> /* (d) */ },
    MatchPhrase { field: String, text: String, slop: u32 /* (d) 0 */ },
    MultiMatch { fields: Vec<(String, f32)>, text: String, kind: MultiMatchKind /* (d) BestFields */, operator: BoolOperator /* (d) Or */,
                 tie_breaker: Option<f32> /* (d) */ },
    Term { field: String, value: FieldValue }, Terms { field: String, values: Vec<FieldValue> },
    Range { field: String, gt: Option<FieldValue>, gte: Option<FieldValue>, lt: Option<FieldValue>, lte: Option<FieldValue> },   // bounds (d)
    Exists { field: String }, IsNull { field: String }, IsEmpty { field: String },
    ValuesCount { field: String, gt: Option<u64>, gte: Option<u64>, lt: Option<u64>, lte: Option<u64> },                        // bounds (d)
    Prefix { field: String, value: String }, Wildcard { field: String, pattern: String },
    Fuzzy { field: String, value: String, fuzziness: Fuzziness },
    Ids(Vec<PrimaryKey>),
    QueryString { query: String, default_fields: Vec<String> /* (d) */, default_operator: BoolOperator /* (d) Or */ },
    Bool { must: Vec<Query>, should: Vec<Query>, must_not: Vec<Query>, filter: Vec<Query>, minimum_should_match: Option<String> },  // all (d)
    Boost { query: Box<Query>, boost: f32 }, ConstantScore { query: Box<Query>, score: f32 },
}
#[derive(Copy, Default)] pub enum BoolOperator { #[default] Or, And }
#[derive(Copy, Default)] pub enum MultiMatchKind { #[default] BestFields, MostFields, CrossFields, Phrase, PhrasePrefix }
#[derive(Copy)] pub enum Fuzziness { Auto, Edits(u8) }                       // JSON: "auto" | 0 | 1 | 2
pub enum FieldValue { Str(String), I64(i64), U64(u64), F64(f64), Bool(bool), Date(i64 /* µs since the epoch, UTC */) }
impl FieldValue { pub fn normalized(self) -> Self; }                       // U64(n <= i64::MAX) → I64(n)
pub enum SortKey { Score { order: SortOrder /* (d) Desc */ }, Pk { order: SortOrder /* (d) Asc */ },
                   Field { field: String, order: SortOrder /* (d) Asc */, missing: MissingOrder /* (d) Last */ } }
#[derive(Copy)] pub enum SortOrder { Asc, Desc }   #[derive(Copy)] pub enum MissingOrder { First, Last }
pub enum SortValue { Null, Bool(bool), I64(i64), U64(u64), F64(f64), Str(String), Uuid([u8; 16]) }
impl SortValue { pub fn normalized(self) -> Self; pub fn as_pk(&self) -> Option<PrimaryKey>; pub fn from_pk(pk: &PrimaryKey) -> Self; }
pub struct Highlight { pub fields: Vec<HighlightField> }
pub struct HighlightField { pub field: String, pub pre_tag: String /* (d) "<em>" */, pub post_tag: String /* (d) "</em>" */,
                            pub fragment_size: usize /* (d) 100 */, pub number_of_fragments: usize /* (d) 5 */ }
pub struct GroupBy { pub field: String, pub group_size: usize /* (d) 3 */, pub limit: usize /* (d) 10 */ }
pub struct HitGroup { pub key: FieldValue, pub hits: Vec<Hit> }
#[derive(Default)] pub enum TrackTotalHits { #[default] None, Exact, UpTo(u64) }
pub struct TotalHits { pub value: u64, pub relation: TotalRelation }  #[derive(Copy)] pub enum TotalRelation { Eq, Gte }
pub struct SearchResponse { pub hits: Vec<Hit>, pub total: Option<TotalHits>, pub aggregations: Option<serde_json::Value>,
                            pub groups: Option<Vec<HitGroup>>, pub read_token: ConsistencyToken,
                            pub hot_used: BTreeSet<HotKind> /* (d), omitted from JSON when empty */ }
pub struct Hit { pub pk: PrimaryKey, pub score: f32, pub sort_values: Vec<SortValue>, pub source: Option<Map<String, Value>>,
                 pub vectors: BTreeMap<String, Vec<f32>>, pub sparse_vectors: BTreeMap<String, SparseVector> /* (d), omitted when empty */,
                 pub highlight: BTreeMap<String, Vec<String>>,
                 pub fields: BTreeMap<String, Vec<FieldValue>> /* (d), omitted when empty */ }

#[derive(Default)] pub enum SourceFilter { #[default] All, None, Paths { include: Vec<String>, exclude: Vec<String> } }

// error.rs
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum ServiceError {
    #[error("{kind} {name:?} not found")] NotFound { kind: &'static str, name: String },
    #[error("already exists: {0}")] AlreadyExists(String),
    #[error("invalid argument: {0}")] InvalidArgument(String),
    #[error("schema violation on {field}: {message}")] SchemaViolation { field: String, message: String },
    #[error("unavailable: {0}")] Unavailable(String),
    #[error("timed out")] Timeout,
    #[error("internal error: {0}")] Internal(String),
}
pub const NOT_FOUND_KINDS: [&str; 9] = ["namespace", "collection", "alias", "pin", "document", "field", "vector", "stream", "object"];
impl ServiceError {
    pub fn code(&self) -> &'static str;        // not_found | already_exists | invalid_argument | schema_violation | unavailable | timeout | internal
    pub fn http_status(&self) -> u16;          // 404 | 409 | 400 | 400 | 503 | 504 | 500
    pub fn is_retryable(&self) -> bool;        // Unavailable | Timeout
    pub fn to_body(&self) -> serde_json::Value;                       // rule 6
    pub fn from_body(body: &serde_json::Value) -> Option<Self>;
}
impl Serialize for ServiceError; impl<'de> Deserialize<'de> for ServiceError;  // via to_body / from_body

                          pub max_query_clauses: usize /* 1_024 */ }

             pub mod vec { /* Vec<PrimaryKey> */ }  pub mod opt { /* Option<PrimaryKey> */ }
             pub fn to_json(pk: &PrimaryKey) -> Value; pub fn from_json(v: &Value) -> Result<PrimaryKey, ServiceError>; }

                 pub fn from_json(v: &Value) -> Result<CollectionSchema, ServiceError>; pub fn field_from_json(v: &Value) -> Result<FieldSpec, ServiceError>;
                 pub fn vector_from_json(v: &Value) -> Result<VectorSpec, ServiceError>;
                 pub mod distance { /* Option<Distance> as "cosine" | "dot" | "euclid" | "manhattan" | null */ } }
// Every IR/service field holding an M1.1 type whose derived serde is a wire form (PrimaryKey, CollectionSchema, Distance,

// hot.rs — the FIXED CONTRACT (verbatim) plus additions
pub trait HotTier: Send + Sync + std::fmt::Debug {
    /// An ANN index for `column` reflecting manifest `source_version <= manifest_version`; None → durable path.
    fn ann(&self, ns: NamespaceId, cid: CollectionId, column: &str, manifest_version: u64) -> Option<Arc<dyn HotAnn>>;
    /// A local file holding the whole split when it is pinned on this node; None → range reads through the cache.
    fn split_file(&self, ns: NamespaceId, cid: CollectionId, split: ulid::Ulid) -> Option<std::path::PathBuf>;
    /// Access accounting for promotion (called once per read of a collection).
    fn record_access(&self, ns: NamespaceId, cid: CollectionId);
    /// Addition (provided): the hot status reported by `GET …/collections/{c}`.
    fn status(&self, _ns: NamespaceId, _cid: CollectionId) -> HotStatus { HotStatus::default() }
}
#[async_trait::async_trait]
pub trait HotAnn: Send + Sync + std::fmt::Debug {
    fn source_version(&self) -> u64;
    /// Row ids covered by the artifact; rows outside are searched on the durable path and merged.
    fn covered(&self) -> &roaring::RoaringTreemap;
    /// Approximate top-k over covered rows, restricted to `allow` when given; scores are approximate (the caller rescores exactly, R12).
    async fn search(&self, query: &[f32], k: usize, allow: Option<&roaring::RoaringTreemap>, ef: Option<u32>) -> Result<Vec<(u64 /* row id */, f32)>, HotError>;
}
#[derive(Debug, Default)] pub struct NoHotTier;   // returns None everywhere
#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)] pub enum HotError { #[error("hot artifact unavailable: {0}")] Unavailable(String), #[error("hot artifact failed: {0}")] Failed(String) }
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash, Serialize, Deserialize)] #[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
pub enum HotKind { Hnsw, Splits }                  // declaration order = sorted header order; fragment prefetch is H1 and never reported (overview A19)
#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)] pub struct HotStatus { pub vectors: HotState, pub text: HotState, pub fragments: HotState }
// The `hot` value of `CollectionInfo`. M1.3's `GET …/collections/{c}` handler replaces it with the owner's full status (M1.3 Task 8), a superset
// whose `vectors`, `text` and `fragments` objects each carry these `state` and `source_version` keys.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)] pub struct HotState { pub state: HotStateKind, pub source_version: Option<u64> }
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)] #[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")] pub enum HotStateKind { #[default] Off, Building, Ready }
#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default)] pub struct HotUsed(/* Arc<Mutex<BTreeSet<HotKind>>> */);
impl HotUsed { pub fn record(&self, kind: HotKind); pub fn kinds(&self) -> BTreeSet<HotKind>; pub fn header_value(&self) -> String; }  // "hnsw,splits" | "none"
#[derive(Clone, Debug)] pub struct RequestHot { pub enabled: bool, pub used: HotUsed }
pub async fn scope<F: Future>(hot: RequestHot, fut: F) -> F::Output;      // tokio::task_local! HOT_SCOPE
pub fn current() -> Option<RequestHot>;
pub const HOT_HEADER: &str = "operon-hot";  pub const HOT_USED_HEADER: &str = "operon-hot-used";
pub fn parse_hot_header(value: &str) -> Result<bool, ServiceError>;       // "on" → true, "off" → false (ASCII case-insensitive); else InvalidArgument
#[derive(Clone, Debug)] pub struct HotLayer { default_enabled: bool }
impl HotLayer { pub fn new(default_enabled: bool) -> Self; }
impl<S> tower::Layer<S> for HotLayer { type Service = HotService<S>; }
#[derive(Clone, Debug)] pub struct HotService<S> { inner: S, default_enabled: bool }
impl<S, B, R> tower::Service<http::Request<B>> for HotService<S> where S: tower::Service<http::Request<B>, Response = http::Response<R>> + Clone + Send + 'static, … ;

// placement.rs — the FIXED CONTRACT (verbatim) plus two helpers
pub trait Placement: Send + Sync + std::fmt::Debug { fn owner(&self, ns: NamespaceId, cid: CollectionId) -> Owner; }
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)] pub enum Owner { Local, Remote { node_id: u64, addr: std::net::SocketAddr } }
#[async_trait::async_trait]
pub trait RemoteReads: Send + Sync + std::fmt::Debug {   // forwards a read to its owner; M1.3 implements over an internal HTTP route.
    // An implementation forwards `hot::current()`'s `enabled` flag and records the owner's reported `Operon-Hot-Used` kinds into `current().used`.
    async fn search(&self, to: &Owner, ns: &str, req: SearchRequest) -> Result<SearchResponse, ServiceError>;
    async fn get(&self, to: &Owner, ns: &str, name: &str, pks: Vec<PrimaryKey>, select: Projection, consistency: ReadConsistency) -> Result<Vec<Option<StoredDoc>>, ServiceError>;
    async fn count(&self, to: &Owner, ns: &str, name: &str, filter: Option<Query>, consistency: ReadConsistency) -> Result<u64, ServiceError>;
    async fn scroll(&self, to: &Owner, ns: &str, name: &str, filter: Option<Query>, after: Option<PrimaryKey>, limit: usize, select: Projection, consistency: ReadConsistency) -> Result<(Vec<StoredDoc>, Option<PrimaryKey>), ServiceError>;
}
#[derive(Debug, Default)] pub struct LocalOnly;       // Placement → always Local
#[derive(Debug, Default)] pub struct NoRemoteReads;   // every method → Unavailable("no remote read transport is configured")
pub const FORWARDED_HEADER: &str = "operon-forwarded";  // set by RemoteReads implementations; the receiving route calls the *_local methods
```

**Semantics:**
1. **Serde shapes** (everything the native API, M1.3 forwarding and the SDKs exchange):
   - Structs are JSON objects with the Rust field names. Enums are externally tagged in `snake_case`: a unit variant is a string (`"match_all"`, `"dbsf"`), a newtype or struct variant a one-key object (`{"ids": [5]}`, `{"rrf": {"k": 60}}`). `Option::None` serializes as `null` and a missing key reads as the default.
   - `Retriever::Rescore.input` is a nested object. `MultiMatch.fields` is `[[name, boost], …]`.
   - `Retriever::Sparse` is `{"sparse": {"field": str, "query": {"indices": [u32], "values": [f32]}, "k": usize, "filter": Query|null, "params": {"idf_corpus": Query|null}}}`. A sparse vector whose lengths differ, whose indices repeat or whose values are not finite fails to deserialize (`SparseVector`'s `try_from`); the REST layer reports it as `InvalidArgument`. Indices may arrive unsorted and are sorted.
   - `TrackTotalHits` is `"none" | "exact" | {"up_to": n}`. `TotalHits` is `{"value": n, "relation": "eq" | "gte"}`. `ReadConsistency` is `"strong" | "eventual" | {"at_least": "<token>"} | {"pinned": {"manifest_version": n, "token": "<token>"}}`.
   - A `ConsistencyToken` is always its text form (`v1:s7/p3@918274`).
   - `SearchResponse.hot_used`, `Hit.fields`, `Hit.sparse_vectors` and `StoredDoc.sparse_vectors` are omitted when empty (`skip_serializing_if`). JSON is the only format these types are encoded in, so skipping is safe; postcard never sees them.
2. **Hand-written forms:**
   - `PrimaryKey`: an integer in `0..=2^64−1` is `U64`, a string is `Str`, and `{"uuid": "<canonical 8-4-4-4-12, any case>"}` is `Uuid`; output is lowercase hyphenated. `true`, `1.5`, `-1`, `null` and other objects are errors (A12).
   - `FieldValue`: a string is `Str`, a bool is `Bool`, an integer is `I64` (or `U64` above `i64::MAX`), a number with a fraction or exponent is `F64`, and `{"date": "<RFC 3339>"}` is `Date` (µs; output `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS.ffffffZ`, always 6 fraction digits).
   - `SortValue`: `null`, bool, integers as for `FieldValue`, float, string, and `{"uuid": …}`.
   - `Fuzziness`: `"auto"` or an integer 0–2.
   - `SourceFilter`: `"all"`, `"none"` or `{"include": [..], "exclude": [..]}` (either list may be missing).
   - `normalized()` turns `U64(n ≤ i64::MAX)` into `I64(n)`. Every value the service emits is normalized, so emitted JSON round-trips exactly.
   - `SortValue::as_pk` maps `I64(n ≥ 0)` and `U64` to `U64`, `Str` to `Str` and `Uuid` to `Uuid`, else `None`. `from_pk` is its inverse (normalized).
3. **Non-finite numbers:** deserialization of the IR accepts them only where serde_json can produce them, which it cannot. `validate_request` rejects any non-finite `f32`/`f64` (vectors, boosts, weights, thresholds, `FieldValue::F64`, `tie_breaker`, `oversampling`).
4. **`validate_request`** returns `InvalidArgument` with exactly these messages, checked in this order:
   1. `"collection must not be empty"`.
   2. `"at most {max_retrievers} retrievers"` — counted over nested inputs too.
   3. `"fused retrievers nest at most {max_fused_depth} deep"`.
   4. `"a fused retriever needs at least one input"`.
   5. `"fusion is required with several retrievers"`.
   6. `"weighted_sum needs one weight per list, got {w} for {n}"` — lists = the retrievers of that level.
   7. `"k must be between 1 and {max_window}"` — every retriever.
   8. `"offset + limit must be at most {max_window}"`.
   9. `"a distance override needs exact search"` — `AnnParams.distance` set without `exact`.
   10. `"a score sort needs a retriever"`.
   11. `"a field sort allows at most one retriever, and it must be text"` — Ruling 6.
   12. `"search_after has {m} values but the sort has {n} keys"` — when `m > n`, where `n` counts the implicit PK key.
   13. `"group_size and limit of group_by must be at least 1"`.
   14. `"aggregations are not a valid aggregation request: {serde error}"` — the value must deserialize into `tantivy::aggregation::agg_req::Aggregations`.
   15. `"non-finite number in {path}"`.
   16. `"a query has more than {max_query_clauses} clauses"` — every `Query` node counts.

   Vector dimensions, field existence and field kinds are checked at execution time against the schema (Tasks 2, 5–7), as are sparse field existence and `idf_corpus` on a field without the `Idf` modifier (Task 6). Rules 7 and 15 cover `Retriever::Sparse` (`k`, and the finite values its `SparseVector` already guarantees).
5. **Schema JSON** (the DTOs behind `json::schema`):
   - Schema `{"version"?: u64 (output only), "fields": [Field], "vectors": [Vector], "dynamic"?: "strict"|"ignore"|"map" (default "strict"), "max_fields"?: u32 (1000), "annotations"?: {str: str} ({})}`.
   - Field `{"name", "source_path"? (default: name), "kind": Kind, "indexed"? (true), "fast"? (false), "ignore_malformed"? (false)}`.
   - Kind `{"text": {"analyzer"? ("standard"), "positions"? (true)}} | "keyword" | "i64" | "f64" | "bool" | "date" | "uuid" | "json"`.
   - `"sparse_vectors"?: [SparseVector] ([])`, SparseVector `{"name", "modifier"? ("none"|"idf", default "none")}` (overview A26).
   - Vector `{"name", "dim", "distance": "cosine"|"dot"|"euclid"|"manhattan", "element"? ("f32"), "index"? (Index, "auto"), "hnsw"? ({"m": 16, "ef_construct": 100, "full_scan_threshold_kb": 10000, "payload_m": null, "on_disk": false}; each key optional with that default), "quantization"? (null)}`.
   - Index `"auto" | "none" | {"ivf_pq": {"num_partitions"?, "num_sub_vectors"?, "num_bits"? (8)}} | {"ivf_rq": {"num_partitions"?, "num_bits"? (1)}} | {"ivf_hnsw_sq": {"num_partitions"?}}`.
   - Quantization `{"scalar": {"quantile_ppm"?, "always_ram"? (false)}} | {"product": {"compression_ratio", "always_ram"?}} | {"binary": {"always_ram"?}}`.
   - Output always carries every key. Input ignores `version` (the service sets it). Unknown keys are `InvalidArgument("unknown key {k} in {what}")`, so a typo is not silently a default.
6. **`ServiceError` body:** `{"error": code(), "message": Display}`, plus:
   - `"kind"` and `"name"` for `NotFound`;
   - `"field"` for `SchemaViolation`;
   - `"id"` for nothing.

   `from_body` maps a `kind` in `NOT_FOUND_KINDS` to that static string and any other kind to `"object"`. It returns `None` for an unknown code.
7. **Hot scope:**
   - `scope` runs `fut` inside a `tokio::task_local!` `HOT_SCOPE`. `current()` returns a clone inside it and `None` outside.
   - `HotService::call`:
     1. Read header `operon-hot`. When absent, `enabled = default_enabled`; when invalid, return 400 with body `{"error":"invalid_argument","message":"invalid Operon-Hot header: <value> (expected on or off)"}` without calling the inner service.
     2. Create `RequestHot { enabled, used: HotUsed::default() }`.
     3. Await the inner call inside `scope`.
     4. Set the response header `operon-hot-used` to `used.header_value()`, unless the inner service already set it (a gateway that forwarded the request).

     Header names match ASCII case-insensitively, so gRPC metadata `operon-hot` is the same header.
   - `header_value` joins the recorded kinds' snake-case names in `HotKind` order with `,`, or gives `none`.
8. **§05 §4 hybrid body** (`json::hybrid::parse_query_body`). A body is the hybrid form iff it has the key `from` or `retrieve`; otherwise it is a `SearchRequest`. The hybrid form:
   - `from`: `"collections.<name>"` or `"<name>"` → `collection`.
   - `consistency`: `"strong"`, `"eventual"` or `{"token": "<token>"}` (→ `AtLeast`). The token prefix must be `v1:`; §05's example `c1:` is refused.
   - `retrieve`: a list of:
     - `{"vector": {"field", "query", "k", "exact"?, "nprobes"?, "refine_factor"?, "ef"?}}` → `Retriever::Vector`;
     - `{"text": {"field", "query": str, "k", "operator"?: "or"|"and"}}` → `Retriever::Text { query: Match { field, text: query, operator, … } }`.
   - `filter`: F, where F is one of:
     - `{"and": [F]}`, `{"or": [F]}`, `{"not": F}` → `Bool` must / should / must_not;
     - `{"term": {field: value}}`, `{"terms": {field: [values]}}`;
     - `{"range": {field: {"gt"|"gte"|"lt"|"lte": value}}}`;
     - `{"exists": field}`, `{"ids": [Id]}`, `{"match": {field: text}}`.

     A value is a JSON scalar, or a date-math string `now`, `now-<n><u>` or `now+<n><u>` with `u ∈ {s, m, h, d}`, which becomes `FieldValue::Date((now_ms ± n·unit) × 1000)`.
   - `fuse`: `{"method": "rrf", "k"?: 60}`, `{"method": "dbsf"}` or `{"method": "weighted", "weights": [f32]}`.
   - `select`: a list of names:
     - `"id"` and `"_score"` are always present, so they are ignored;
     - `"_source"` → `SourceFilter::All`;
     - `"_vectors.<name>"` → `vectors`;
     - every other name → `SourceFilter::Paths { include }`;
     - an empty list → `None`.
   - `limit` (10), `offset` (0).
   - Unsupported keys: `expand` → `InvalidArgument("expand needs graphs, which arrive in M3")`; `rerank` → `InvalidArgument("rerank arrives in M3")` (the rerank stage of D44; the SQL `rerank` refuses the same way, Ruling 23).

   Any other unknown key is `InvalidArgument("unknown key {k} in the query body")`.

**Tests** (`tests/it/ir_json.rs`):
- `sparse_retrievers_use_the_json_form`: `{"sparse": {"field": "s", "query": {"indices": [5, 1], "values": [0.5, 2.0]}, "k": 3}}` deserializes with indices `[1, 5]`, values `[2.0, 0.5]`, `filter: None` and default params, and serializes back with sorted indices and every key; `{"indices": [1, 1], "values": [1, 2]}` and `{"indices": [1], "values": []}` are errors; a `Hit` without sparse vectors has no `sparse_vectors` key.
- `ir_json_round_trips` (proptest, 512 cases): random `SearchRequest`s cover every `Query` and `Retriever` variant (`Sparse` included) to depth 3; `FieldValue`/`SortValue` include `U64(u64::MAX)`, uuids and dates; `f32` values are finite. `from_value(to_value(r))` must equal `r.normalized()`. The same holds for `SearchResponse`, `StoredDoc`, `CollectionInfo`, `WriteResult` and `ServiceError`.
- `the_wire_form_matches_the_fixture`: `data/hybrid_request.json` is the M1.6 fixture step 12 body: vector plus text retrievers, `{"rrf": {"k": 60}}`, `limit` 3, every other key at its default. It deserializes to exactly the expected `SearchRequest`. `serde_json::to_value` of that request equals the file's value (same keys and values).
- `primary_keys_use_the_a12_json_form`: `5` → `U64(5)`, `"5"` → `Str("5")`, `{"uuid":"0190F5C4-6C1E-7B3A-9D2E-4F5A6B7C8D9E"}` → `Uuid(..)`, which writes back lowercase, and `18446744073709551615` → `U64(u64::MAX)`. `true`, `1.5`, `-1`, `null` and `{"x":1}` are each an error.
- `schema_json_defaults_follow_the_sdk_contract`: for `{"fields":[{"name":"body","kind":{"text":{"analyzer":"english"}}}],"vectors":[{"name":"e","dim":3,"distance":"cosine"}]}`:
  - `source_path` is `"body"`, `indexed` true, `fast` false, `positions` true;
  - `dynamic` is `Strict`, `max_fields` 1000;
  - the vector has `element` F32, `index` Auto, default HNSW and no quantization; `sparse_vectors` is `[]`, and `{"sparse_vectors": [{"name": "s"}]}` gives modifier `None`;
  - the serialized form carries every key of rule 5.

  `{"fields":[{"name":"x","kind":"keyword","fats":true}]}` gives `InvalidArgument("unknown key fats in field")`.
- `consistency_json_forms`: the four forms round-trip, and `{"at_least":"v1:s01/p0@1"}` is an error.
- `service_errors_round_trip_their_bodies`: every variant round-trips through `to_body`/`from_body`, with the exact codes and HTTP statuses of the Produces block, and `NotFound { kind: "pin", .. }` keeps its kind.
- `validation_messages_are_exact`: one invalid request per rule 4 item gives exactly its message.
- `op_result_existed_follows_the_table`.
- `sort_and_field_values_serialize_normalized`: `U64(7)` → `7` → `I64(7)`; `U64(u64::MAX)` round-trips as `U64`; `Date(1_704_153_600_000_000)` ↔ `{"date":"2024-01-02T00:00:00.000000Z"}`.
- `hybrid_body_maps_to_the_ir`: `data/hybrid_body.json` is §05 §4's example without `expand` and `rerank`, with the token `v1:s7/p3@918273`, and `now_ms` fixed at `1_750_000_000_000`. It maps to the expected request, including `range ts gte Date((1_750_000_000_000 − 30·86_400_000) × 1000)`. Adding `expand`, or `rerank`, gives exactly its M3 message.
- `the_hot_scope_is_task_local`: `current()` is `None` outside and `Some` inside, and a `record` inside is visible in the `HotUsed` kept outside.
- `the_hot_layer_parses_the_header_and_reports_used` (a `tower::service_fn` inner service that records `Hnsw` when `current().enabled`):
  - no header → the response has `operon-hot-used: hnsw`;
  - `Operon-Hot: OFF` → `none`, and the inner service saw `enabled == false`;
  - `Operon-Hot: maybe` → 400 with the exact body, and the inner service was not called.
- `no_hot_tier_and_local_only_are_inert`: `NoHotTier` returns `None` everywhere; `LocalOnly.owner(..) == Owner::Local`; every `NoRemoteReads` method returns `Unavailable`.

**Commit:** `query: add operon-query with the search IR and its JSON form`; `query: add the hot-tier and placement hooks`.
