### Task 2: The `Query` compiler — IR `Query` → Tantivy query, per split schema

**Files:** `crates/operon-query/src/text/{mod.rs,fields.rs,coerce.rs,compile.rs}`, `crates/operon-query/tests/compile.rs`.

**Consumes:**
- Task 1: `Query`, `FieldValue`, `BoolOperator`, `MultiMatchKind`, `Fuzziness`, `ServiceError`.
- M1.1: `CollectionSchema`, `FieldSpec`, `FieldKind`, `PK_FIELD`, `FIELD_PRESENCE_FIELD`, the companion-name functions, `parse_date`, `tokenizer_manager()` (Tantivy's; its analyzers are registered into Quickwit's manager, P28, row 0.44), the vendored `WarmupInfo` (fields in row 0.47), `build_query(ast, &BuildTantivyAstContext { schema, tokenizer_manager, search_fields: &[], with_validation: true }, None)` (rows 0.47–0.48), `QueryAst::FieldPresence(FieldPresenceQuery { field })` and `operon_quickwit::query::tokenizers::TokenizerManager`.
- tantivy 0.26.2:
  - `BooleanQuery::{new, set_minimum_number_should_match}`, `TermQuery::new`, `TermSetQuery::new`, `PhraseQuery::new_with_offset_and_slop`, `PhrasePrefixQuery::new_with_offset`;
  - `RangeQuery::new(Bound<Term>, Bound<Term>)`, `ExistsQuery::new(field, json_subpaths)`, `FuzzyTermQuery::new(term, distance, true)`, `RegexQuery::from_pattern`;
  - `DisjunctionMaxQuery::with_tie_breaker`, `BoostQuery::new`, `ConstScoreQuery::new`, `AllQuery`, `EmptyQuery`, `QueryParser`;
  - `Term::{from_field_text, from_field_i64, from_field_u64, from_field_f64, from_field_bool, from_field_date, from_field_bytes}` and the JSON term constructors.

**Produces:**

```rust
// text/mod.rs (P28, row 0.44). `TokenizerManager` below is always this Quickwit type.
/// Quickwit's `TokenizerManager::new()` plus operon-text's five analyzers (from `operon_text::tokenizer_manager().get(name)`),
/// registered with `does_lowercasing` = true for "standard", "english", "simple" and false for "whitespace", "keyword".
pub fn query_tokenizers() -> operon_quickwit::query::tokenizers::TokenizerManager;

// fields.rs
pub enum ResolvedField<'a> {
    Plain { spec: &'a FieldSpec },                     // an exact FieldSpec name
    JsonPath { spec: &'a FieldSpec, path: String },    // "<json field name>.<path>", longest Json field name that is a dot-prefix
    Unknown,
}
pub fn resolve_field<'a>(schema: &'a CollectionSchema, name: &str) -> ResolvedField<'a>;

// coerce.rs
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub enum Coerced { Str(String), I64(i64), F64(f64), Bool(bool), DateMs(i64), Never /* the value can never match this field */ }
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)] pub enum RangeOp { Gt, Gte, Lt, Lte }
/// A term value for `kind`, per rule 4; Err = InvalidArgument with the rule's message.
pub fn coerce_term(kind: &FieldKind, field: &str, value: &FieldValue) -> Result<Coerced, ServiceError>;
/// A range bound for `kind`, per rule 5 (numbers and dates round to the field's precision).
pub fn coerce_bound(kind: &FieldKind, field: &str, op: RangeOp, value: &FieldValue) -> Result<Bound<Coerced>, ServiceError>;
pub fn date_bound_ms(value_us: i64, op: RangeOp) -> Bound<i64>;
pub fn format_date_us(value_us: i64) -> String;   // RFC 3339 with 6 fraction digits and Z

// compile.rs
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)] pub enum CompileMode { Scoring, Filter }
pub struct CompiledQuery { pub query: Box<dyn tantivy::query::Query>, pub warmup: WarmupInfo,
                           pub constant_score: bool /* no leaf computes BM25 */ }
pub struct QueryCompiler<'a> { /* schema, split tantivy schema, tokenizers */ }
impl<'a> QueryCompiler<'a> {
    pub fn new(schema: &'a CollectionSchema, split_schema: &'a tantivy::schema::Schema, tokenizers: &'a TokenizerManager) -> Self;
    pub fn compile(&self, query: &Query, mode: CompileMode) -> Result<CompiledQuery, ServiceError>;
    /// Bool { must: [query], filter: [filter] } without allocating the IR.
    pub fn compile_with_filter(&self, query: &Query, filter: Option<&Query>) -> Result<CompiledQuery, ServiceError>;
}
pub fn parse_minimum_should_match(spec: &str, optional: usize) -> Result<usize, ServiceError>;
pub fn fuzziness_edits(fuzziness: Fuzziness, term: &str) -> u8;
/// (field name, analyzed term text) of every scoring text leaf, for highlighting (Task 8).
pub fn highlight_terms(schema: &CollectionSchema, query: &Query, tokenizers: &TokenizerManager) -> Vec<(String, String)>;
```

**Semantics:**
1. **Field resolution.** Resolution is by name, against both the collection schema (for the kind) and the split's own Tantivy schema (for the `Field`; M1.1 names Tantivy fields after `FieldSpec.name`).
   - A name that equals a `FieldSpec.name` is `Plain`. Otherwise, the longest `Json` field name `n` with `name` starting `n + "."` gives `JsonPath { path: name[n.len()+1..] }`. Otherwise `Unknown`.
   - The reserved name `_id` means the primary key: `Term`/`Terms` on `_id` compile like `Ids`.
   - A `Plain` or `JsonPath` field that the split's schema lacks (a split written before the field was added, A4) compiles to `EmptyQuery` for that split. So does an `Unknown` field.
   - `Exists` on such a field is empty and `IsEmpty` matches all. This is the ES "unmapped field matches nothing" rule.
2. **Scores.**
   - `MatchAll` scores 1.0.
   - `Term` on text, keyword and JSON string fields scores BM25 (a `TermQuery` with frequencies).
   - `Terms`, `Range`, `Exists`, `IsNull`, `IsEmpty`, `ValuesCount`, `Prefix`, `Wildcard`, `Fuzzy`, `Ids` and numeric, bool or date `Term`s score a constant 1.0 (`ConstScoreQuery(q, 1.0)`).
   - `Boost` multiplies. `ConstantScore` replaces the score.
   - In `CompileMode::Filter` every leaf is wrapped in `ConstScoreQuery(q, 0.0)`, and scoring is disabled when the query runs (Task 5).
3. **Per variant:**
   - **`Match`** on a `Text` field:
     1. Analyze `text` with `analyzer` if given, else the field's analyzer; no tokens → `EmptyQuery`.
     2. One `TermQuery` per token (`WithFreqs`), or a `FuzzyTermQuery::new(term, fuzziness_edits(f, token), true)` when `fuzziness` is set.
     3. Combine them: `Or` → all `Should`, with `minimum_should_match` applied through `parse_minimum_should_match(msm, n)`; `And` → all `Must`.

     On `Keyword`/`Uuid`: one term, the exact text (UUID text parsed and lowercased). On a numeric, bool or date field: as `Term` with `FieldValue::Str(text)`. On a JSON path: analyze with `standard` into the `_text.<n>` companion at `path`.
   - **`MatchPhrase`:** tokens with their positions (stop-word gaps kept). One token → `TermQuery`; several → `PhraseQuery::new_with_offset_and_slop(terms, slop)`. A `Text` field indexed without positions → `InvalidArgument("field {f} was indexed without positions")`. A JSON path uses `_text.<n>`.
   - **`MultiMatch`:** per field `f` with boost `b`, `q_f` = `Match` (or `MatchPhrase` for `Phrase`, or phrase-prefix for `PhrasePrefix`) wrapped in `BoostQuery(q_f, b)`.
     - `BestFields`, `Phrase` and `PhrasePrefix`: `DisjunctionMaxQuery::with_tie_breaker(q_f…, tie_breaker or 0.0)`.
     - `MostFields`: all `Should` (a sum).
     - `CrossFields`: term-centric. Each field's analyzer yields its token list. For every distinct token `t`, a `DisjunctionMaxQuery` over `BoostQuery(Term(f, t), b)` for the fields that produced `t`, with `tie_breaker`. The per-token queries combine by `operator` (`Or` = Should, `And` = Must). IDF is not blended across fields.
     - `PhrasePrefix` of a single token is a prefix term query (rule on `Prefix`) with the last token as prefix, capped at 50 expansions (`PhrasePrefixQuery::new_with_offset` with `set_max_expansions(50)` for several tokens).
   - **`Term`/`Terms`** on a `Plain` field: `coerce_term`. `Never` → `EmptyQuery`. `Terms` → `TermSetQuery` over the non-`Never` values.
     - On a JSON path `n.path`: type-strict terms on the main field `n` at `path` (S4):
       - `Str` → the raw string term;
       - `I64` → the i64 JSON term only; `U64 > i64::MAX` → the u64 JSON term only; `F64` → the f64 JSON term only;
       - `Bool` → the bool term;
       - `Date` → the `_date.<n>` companion's date term, at ms precision (`EmptyQuery` unless `µs % 1000 == 0`).
   - **`Range`:** each present bound goes through `coerce_bound` into `RangeQuery` bounds on the field's term type.
     - `Text`/`Keyword` compare terms bytewise.
     - A bound that is `Never` on the lower side makes the query empty; on the upper side, likewise.
     - On a JSON path, numeric bounds give `Should[range over i64 terms (bounds rounded as for I64 fields), range over f64 terms, range over u64 terms when a bound exceeds i64::MAX]`. Date bounds use `_date.<n>`; string bounds use raw string terms.
   - **`Exists`:**
     - `Plain` → the vendored `FieldPresence` query for the field (`QueryAst::FieldPresence` through `build_query`), the term M1.1 writes for every non-Json field with a value;
     - `JsonPath` → `ExistsQuery::new(format!("{n}.{path}"), true)`;
     - the whole Json field (`name == n`) → `ExistsQuery::new(n, true)`.
   - **`IsNull`:** JSON paths only, as the term `path` in `_null.<n>`. On a `Plain` field it is `InvalidArgument("is_null is supported on JSON field paths only")`.
   - **`IsEmpty`:** `Bool { must: [AllQuery], must_not: [Exists] }`.
   - **`ValuesCount`:** JSON paths only; else `InvalidArgument("values_count is supported on JSON field paths only")`. It is a u64 range on `_count.<n>` at `path`, OR-ed with `IsEmpty` when the range admits 0.
   - **`Prefix`:** `RegexQuery::from_pattern(escape(value) + ".*", field)`, where `escape` backslash-escapes every regex metacharacter `\.+*?()|[]{}^$#&-~"`. On a `Text` field the value is lowercased when the analyzer lowercases (`standard`, `english`, `simple`: `tokenizers.tokenizer_does_lowercasing(analyzer)`). On a JSON path it applies to the main field's raw strings.
   - **`Wildcard`:** the regex is built by escaping everything except `*` → `.*` and `?` → `.`, with the same lowercasing rule as `Prefix`.
   - **`Fuzzy`:** `FuzzyTermQuery::new(term(value), fuzziness_edits(f, value), true)` (transpositions count 1, as ES's default).
   - **`Ids`:** `TermSetQuery` of `Term::from_field_bytes(_pk, canonical)`.
   - **`QueryString`:** Tantivy `QueryParser::new(split_schema, default_fields resolved (Unknown ones dropped; none left → EmptyQuery), tokenizers.tantivy_manager().clone())`, with `set_conjunction_by_default()` when `default_operator == And`. A parse error is `InvalidArgument("query_string: {error}")`.
   - **`Bool`:**
     - `must` → `Must`; `should` → `Should`; `must_not` → `MustNot`; `filter` → `Must(ConstScoreQuery(q, 0.0))`.
     - With no `must`, `should` or `filter` but some `must_not`, add `Must(ConstScoreQuery(AllQuery, 0.0))`, so a pure `must_not` matches the rest with score 0.
     - An empty `Bool` is `AllQuery`.
     - `minimum_should_match` → `set_minimum_number_should_match(parse(msm, should.len()))`. Without it, the ES default is 1 when there is no `must` and no `filter`, else 0.
   - **`Boost`:** `BoostQuery`. **`ConstantScore`:** `ConstScoreQuery`. **`MatchNone`:** `EmptyQuery`.
4. **`coerce_term(kind, value)`** (M1.5 C15: values follow the field kind):

   | kind | `Str(s)` | `I64(n)`/`U64(n)` | `F64(x)` | `Bool(b)` | `Date(us)` |
   |---|---|---|---|---|---|
   | Text, Keyword | `Str(s)` | `Str(n.to_string())` | `Str(x.to_string())` (Rust shortest form) | `Str("true"/"false")` | `Str(format_date_us(us))` |
   | Uuid | parse → lowercase hyphenated; unparsable → `Never` | `Never` | `Never` | `Never` | `Never` |
   | I64 | parse i64, else parse f64 then as `F64`, else error `"cannot use {s:?} on long field {f}"` | `I64` (`U64 > i64::MAX` → `Never`) | integral and in range → `I64`, else `Never` | error | error |
   | F64 | parse f64 (finite), else error | `F64(n as f64)` | `F64(x)` | error | error |
   | Bool | `"true"`/`"false"` → `Bool`, else error | error | error | `Bool(b)` | error |
   | Date | `parse_date(Value::String(s))` → `DateMs`, else error | `DateMs(n)` (epoch ms, ES) | error | error | `DateMs(us / 1000)` if `us % 1000 == 0`, else `Never` |
   | Json (the whole field) | error `"use a path inside JSON field {f}"` | same | same | same | same |

   Errors are `InvalidArgument` with the quoted message; "error" cells read `"cannot use a {type} value on {kind} field {f}"`.
5. **`coerce_bound(kind, op, value)`:**
   - For `I64` with an `F64(x)` bound:
     - `Gte` → `Included(ceil(x))`;
     - `Gt` → `Excluded(floor(x))`;
     - `Lte` → `Included(floor(x))`;
     - `Lt` → `Excluded(ceil(x))`.

     Values beyond `i64` saturate to "match none" (a lower bound above `i64::MAX`) or "no bound" (a lower bound below `i64::MIN`), and symmetrically for upper bounds.
   - For `Date` with `Date(us)`, `date_bound_ms`:
     - `Gte` → `Included(ceil_div(us, 1000))`;
     - `Gt` → `Excluded(floor_div(us, 1000))`;
     - `Lte` → `Included(floor_div(us, 1000))`;
     - `Lt` → `Excluded(ceil_div(us, 1000))`.

     Here `floor_div` is `div_euclid` and `ceil_div(a, b) = -((-a).div_euclid(b))`. So a µs bound compares exactly against ms-precision values.
   - Other cells follow rule 4, with `Never` replaced by the saturation above.
6. **`parse_minimum_should_match(spec, n)`** (ES syntax):
   - `"3"` → min(3, n); `"-1"` → n − 1;
   - `"75%"` → floor(n × 0.75); `"-25%"` → n − floor(n × 0.25);
   - results are clamped to `0..=n`;
   - anything else, including the `"3<90%"` combination form, is `InvalidArgument("unsupported minimum_should_match {spec}")`.
7. **`fuzziness_edits`:** `Edits(e)` → `min(e, 2)`. `Auto` → 0 for fewer than 3 chars, 1 for 3–5 chars, 2 above (ES `AUTO:3,6`).
8. **Warmup.** `CompiledQuery.warmup` records everything the query reads, so that the vendored `warmup(searcher, &info)` makes a synchronous search possible over the async split directory:
   - the term-dictionary fields of every term, prefix, wildcard, fuzzy and range leaf;
   - every term with `needs_positions` for phrases;
   - the range and automaton leaves;
   - the fast fields used: `_rowid`, `_pk`, and the fast fields behind `Exists` on JSON paths;
   - `field_norms = true` unless `constant_score`.

   The vendored `build_query` result for `FieldPresence` contributes its own `WarmupInfo`, merged in (`WarmupInfo::merge`). In the as-built fields (row 0.47): term leaves go to `terms_grouped_by_field` (the `bool` is "needs positions"), range leaves to `term_ranges_grouped_by_field`, prefix, wildcard and regex leaves to `automatons_grouped_by_field` (`Automaton::Regex(json path bytes or None, pattern)`), fuzzy and `QueryString` fields to `term_dict_fields`, and fast fields to `fast_fields` (`FastFieldWarmupInfo { name, with_subfields }`, `with_subfields: true` for JSON paths). `required_terms` is always left empty (and cleared in what `build_query` returns): a non-empty set lets the vendored `warmup` cancel the other downloads and return "provably empty" (row 0.46), which would leave the split cold for the statistics and fetch reads that follow.
9. **`highlight_terms`:** walk the query. For every `Match`, `MatchPhrase`, `MultiMatch` or `Term` leaf on a `Text` field (or on a JSON path through `_text.<n>`), emit `(field name, token text)` for each analyzed token. `must_not` subtrees are skipped.

**Tests** (`tests/compile.rs`, over one split built with M1.1's `operon_text::build_split(tantivy_layout(schema).schema, docs)` and opened with `open_split` over the vendored `RamStorage`, then `warm_up_all`, from 12 fixed documents with fields `title` Text english, `tag` Keyword fast, `n` I64 fast, `x` F64 fast, `b` Bool fast, `d` Date fast, `u` Uuid, and `payload` Json with `source_path ""`):
- `every_variant_matches_the_expected_docs`: a table of 60 rows `(Query JSON, expected set of doc pks)`, at least two per variant and field kind, including every row of the coercion table.
- `json_paths_are_type_strict` (M1.4 S4): `payload.n = I64(1)` matches `{"n":1}` and not `{"n":"1"}` or `{"n":1.0}`. `Range payload.n gte F64(0.5)` matches both `1` and `1.5`. `payload.tags = Str("a")` matches `{"tags":["a","b"]}`. `payload.o.k = I64(2)` matches `{"o":{"k":[1,2]}}`.
- `json_paths_support_text_and_date_conditions` (M1.4 S11): `Match payload.body "Quick Fox"` matches `{"body": "the quick brown fox"}` (standard analyzer via `_text.payload`), `MatchPhrase payload.body "quick brown"` matches it and `"brown quick"` does not, and `Range payload.when gte Date(2024-01-01)` matches `{"when": "2024-01-02"}` and not `{"when": "yesterday"}`.
- `is_empty_and_is_null_follow_qdrant` (S5):
  - `IsEmpty payload.k` matches `{}`, `{"k":null}` and `{"k":[]}`;
  - `IsNull payload.k` matches `{"k":null}` and `{"k":[1,null]}` but not `{}`;
  - `ValuesCount payload.k lte 0` matches `{}`, and `gte 2` matches `{"k":[1,2]}`.
- `date_bounds_round_to_milliseconds_exactly`: a doc at `d = 1_000 ms`:
  - `gt Date(999_999 µs)` matches;
  - `gt Date(1_000_000)` does not;
  - `gte Date(1_000_001)` does not;
  - `lt Date(1_000_001)` matches;
  - `Term Date(1_000_500)` matches nothing.
- `a_decimal_bound_on_a_long_field_rounds_like_es`: `n ∈ {1, 2}`: `gt 1.5` → {2}, `lt 1.5` → {1}, `gte 2.0` → {2}.
- `minimum_should_match_forms`: `"2"`, `"-1"`, `"75%"`, `"-25%"` over 4 tokens give 2, 3, 3 and 3; `"3<90%"` is an error.
- `fuzziness_auto_follows_es`: `"ab"` → 0, `"abc"` → 1, `"abcdef"` → 2.
- `a_pure_must_not_matches_the_rest_with_score_zero`; `a_filter_only_bool_scores_zero`; `match_all_scores_one` (M1.5's assumptions).
- `multi_match_best_fields_uses_the_tie_breaker`: two fields with known single-field scores `s1 > s2` give `s1 + 0.3·s2` with `tie_breaker 0.3`, to 1e-6.
- `an_unknown_field_matches_nothing_and_is_empty_matches_all`.
- `a_split_written_before_a_field_existed_matches_nothing_on_it`: a second split built with a schema lacking `tag`: `Term tag` is empty there, and `IsEmpty tag` matches all of it.
- `a_phrase_on_a_field_without_positions_is_invalid`; `is_null_on_a_plain_field_is_invalid`.
- `query_string_honours_the_default_operator`.
- `highlight_terms_skip_must_not`.

**Commit:** `query: compile the query IR to Tantivy queries`.

### Task 3: The tail index (H3)

**Files:**
- `crates/operon-query/src/tail/{mod.rs,index.rs,apply.rs,follower.rs,snapshot.rs}`, `crates/operon-query/tests/tail.rs`;
- `crates/operon-log/src/reader.rs`, `crates/operon-log/tests/fetch_ranges.rs` (new).

**Consumes:**
- M1.1: `decode` (crate root, row 0.20), `partition_of`, `fold`, `apply_patch`, `check_document`, `tantivy_layout`, `to_tantivy_doc`, `tokenizer_manager()`, `live_manifest`, `ManifestCache`, `decode_pk_delta`, `CollectionSnapshot::get_by_pk` (row ids and documents), `CollectionContext`, `Collection`; in tests, `fold_stream(schema, partitions, records)` (crate root, X15, row 0.65) and the link source (`LinkApplySource` with `CollectionTargetFactory`).
- As built: `LogReader::fetch(FetchRequest)`, `FetchResponse`, `LogError::OffsetOutOfRange`, `MetaStore::{collection_head, watch_changes}` (M1.2a; `CollectionHead.high_watermarks` replaces M1.1's `PartitionState::high_watermark`), and `partition_index` behind `LogReader`.
- tantivy: `Index::create_in_ram`, `IndexWriter::{delete_term, add_document, commit}`, `IndexReader` with `ReloadPolicy::Manual`, `Searcher::space_usage`.

**Produces:**

```rust
pub const TAIL_ROWID_BASE: u64 = 1 << 63;
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct TailConfig {
    pub max_bytes: usize,            // 256 MiB per collection
    pub total_max_bytes: usize,      // 2 GiB per process (TailBudget)
    pub idle_ttl: Duration,          // 10 min without a read → the tail stops
    pub follow_interval: Duration,   // 250 ms (the follower also wakes on watch_changes and notify)
    pub fetch_bytes: usize,          // 4 MiB per FetchRequest
    pub compact_garbage_ratio: f64,  // 0.5
    pub compact_min_entries: usize,  // 10_000
    pub writer_memory: usize,        // 32 MiB Tantivy writer arena
    pub resolve_batch: usize,        // 1_000 keys per durable lookup
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct TailDoc { pub row_id: u64, pub pk: PrimaryKey, pub partition: u32, pub offset: u64,
                     pub doc: Option<Arc<Document>> /* None = deleted */ }
#[derive(Clone, Debug)] pub enum TailLookup { Present(Arc<TailDoc>), Deleted(Arc<TailDoc>), Absent }
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)] pub enum TailState { Following, Overflow { head: BTreeMap<u32, u64> }, Failed(String) }
#[derive(Debug)] pub struct TailSnapshot { /* see rule 2 */ }
impl TailSnapshot {
    pub fn empty(manifest_path: Option<String>, manifest: Arc<CollectionManifest>, schema_version: u64) -> Arc<Self>;
    pub fn manifest(&self) -> &Arc<CollectionManifest>;
    pub fn manifest_path(&self) -> Option<&str>;
    pub fn head(&self) -> &BTreeMap<u32, u64>;          // per partition: every offset below is folded in
    pub fn schema_version(&self) -> u64;
    pub fn searcher(&self) -> Option<&tantivy::Searcher>;   // None when no overlay doc is present
    pub fn live(&self) -> &RoaringTreemap;               // row ids of present overlay docs
    pub fn shadow(&self) -> &RoaringTreemap;             // durable row ids superseded by overlay entries (present or deleted)
    pub fn live_count(&self) -> u64;
    pub fn get(&self, pk: &PrimaryKey) -> TailLookup;
    pub fn doc(&self, row_id: u64) -> Option<Arc<TailDoc>>;
    pub fn keys_after(&self, after: Option<&PrimaryKey>, limit: usize) -> Vec<(PrimaryKey, TailLookup)>;   // overlay keys in PK order
    pub fn live_docs(&self) -> Vec<Arc<TailDoc>>;        // present overlay docs, in row-id order
    pub fn bytes(&self) -> usize;
}
#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum TailError {
    #[error("the tail is over its memory bound at {head:?}")] Overflow { head: BTreeMap<u32, u64> },
    #[error("partition {partition} is trimmed below {offset} (log start {log_start})")] Trimmed { partition: u32, offset: u64, log_start: u64 },
    #[error("the tail did not reach {target:?} in time")] Timeout { target: BTreeMap<u32, u64> },
    #[error("tail: {0}")] Log(#[from] LogError),
    #[error("tail: {0}")] Collection(#[from] CollectionError),
    #[error("the tail stopped")] Stopped,
}
#[derive(Debug, Default)] pub struct TailBudget { /* total_max_bytes, used: AtomicUsize */ }
#[derive(Debug)] pub struct Tail { /* follower handle, published: RwLock<Arc<TailSnapshot>>, state, notify: tokio::sync::Notify */ }
impl Tail {
    pub fn start(ns: NamespaceId, collection: Collection, ctx: CollectionContext, reader: LogReader,
                 config: TailConfig, budget: Arc<TailBudget>) -> Arc<Self>;
    pub fn current(&self) -> Arc<TailSnapshot>;
    pub async fn sync(&self, targets: &BTreeMap<u32, u64>, deadline: tokio::time::Instant) -> Result<Arc<TailSnapshot>, TailError>;
    pub fn notify(&self);
    pub fn state(&self) -> TailState;
    pub fn last_read(&self) -> Instant;
    pub async fn stop(&self);
    #[cfg(feature = "test-util")] pub fn pause_fetch(&self, paused: bool);   // holds the follower before its next fetch
}
/// Builds a tail over exactly (snapshot manifest's applied, upper] (Ruling 1), without a follower.
pub async fn build_range_tail(ns: NamespaceId, collection: &Collection, ctx: &CollectionContext, reader: &LogReader,
    durable: &CollectionSnapshot, upper: &BTreeMap<u32, u64>, max_bytes: usize) -> Result<Arc<TailSnapshot>, TailError>;
```

**Semantics:**
1. **State**, one tail per collection with per-partition offsets:
   - `manifest` (path and `Arc<CollectionManifest>`, M_T) and `applied = M_T.applied` (absent partition = 0), plus `head[p]`: every record of partition *p* below `head[p]` is folded in.
   - A *generation*: an append-only `Vec<TailDoc>` (chunked, so appends never move earlier docs), a RAM Tantivy index built with `tantivy_layout(current schema)`, `set_tokenizers(tokenizer_manager())` and `set_fast_field_tokenizers(tokenizer_manager())` (as `build_split` does, row 0.63) and one writer thread with `writer_memory`, and `seq_next`.
   - Writer-side maps:
     - `latest: BTreeMap<PrimaryKey, u32>`, the index of each overlay key's latest entry, shared with snapshots behind an `RwLock`;
     - `prev: Vec<Option<u32>>`, per entry the index of the previous entry for the same key;
     - `shadow_of: HashMap<PrimaryKey, Option<u64>>`, per overlay key the durable row id of the key in M_T.
   - The bitmaps `live_now` and `shadow_now`.
   - The invariant: every overlay key's latest entry has `offset >= applied[p]`.
2. **Snapshots.** After each applied batch or adoption the writer:
   1. commits the Tantivy writer (skipped when nothing was added or deleted);
   2. reloads its reader;
   3. publishes `TailSnapshot { manifest, head (clone), searcher, docs (Arc of the generation + len), latest (Arc), live (clone of live_now), shadow (clone of shadow_now), live_count, schema_version, bytes }`.

   Readers take `current()` (one `Arc` clone). A snapshot's `get(pk)`:
   1. `i = latest[pk]` (read lock);
   2. follow `prev` from `i` until an entry with index `< len`;
   3. if found and the entry is not covered (`offset >= manifest.applied[p]`), it is `Present` or `Deleted` by its `doc`; otherwise `Absent`.

   `keys_after` iterates the `latest` map's range after `after`, applies `get` to each key, and skips `Absent`. Tantivy's snapshot isolation keeps an old searcher valid while the writer goes on (Review Focus 1).
3. **Applying one record** (`apply.rs`), partition *p*, offset *o*:
   1. `decode`. An error means the link dead-letters it too: skip it; `head[p] = o + 1`. So is a record whose `partition_of(pk, collection.partitions) != p` (M1.1 P8, row 0.54).
   2. `k = op.pk()`. `before` = the overlay state if `k` has an overlay entry (`Present` → `Some(doc)`, `Deleted` → `None`); otherwise the durable state of `k` in M_T (rule 4), `None` when absent.
   3. `after = fold(before, [op])` (M1.1 `fold`, so a patch of a missing key without `upsert` leaves it absent).
   4. If `after` is `Some(d)` and `check_document(schema with dynamic = Ignore, d)` fails, skip the op: the link dead-letters it (M1.1 Task 10 rule 3.5). The state stays `before`.
   5. If the op is not an `Upsert` and `after == before` (equal `pk`, `source`, `vectors` and `sparse_vectors`; a patch that changes nothing, a patch of a missing key without `upsert`, a delete of an absent key): nothing changes, and no entry is appended. The link writes no row for such an op either, so `seq_no` stays that of the last write. An `Upsert` always goes to step 6, even when it equals `before`: the link writes a new row and `seq_no` for every upsert (M1.1 P32, X3, row 0.53).
   6. Otherwise:
      - append `TailDoc { row_id: TAIL_ROWID_BASE + seq_next, pk: k, partition: p, offset: o, doc: after }` with `prev = latest.get(k)`, and set `latest[k]`;
      - `live_now`: remove the previous entry's row id if it was present, and add the new row id if `after` is `Some`;
      - Tantivy: `delete_term(Term::from_field_bytes(_pk, k.canonical()))`, then `add_document(to_tantivy_doc(layout, schema, &d, &extracted, row_id))` if present;
      - if `k` was not in the overlay: `shadow_of[k] = durable row id of k in M_T` (rule 4), inserted into `shadow_now` when `Some`.
   7. `head[p] = o + 1`.
4. **Durable resolution.**
   - Before applying a fetched batch, collect the keys of its ops that have no overlay entry. Resolve them in chunks of `resolve_batch` with `CollectionSnapshot::get_by_pk` on M_T's snapshot (M1.1's `CollectionSnapshot::at`, cached per manifest version by `Reads`, Task 4). That gives `(row_id, Document)` per key.
   - M_T with `lance_version == 0` has no rows.
   - Resolution is exact for M_T by construction. It needs no PK index and no watermark.
5. **Follower loop** (`follower.rs`), one tokio task per tail. Each iteration:
   1. Wait for the first of: a `notify()`, `changed()` on a `MetaChanges` from `ctx.meta.watch_changes()` (created before the previous step 2, so no change is missed), or `follow_interval`. Once `changed()` returns `Err(MetaStopped)`, later iterations wait only for `notify()` or `follow_interval` (row 0.79).
   2. `collection_head(Local, cid)` gives the collection record (schema version), the pointer `collection/<cid>` and every partition's high watermark; `None` means the collection was dropped, which stops the tail (`Stopped`).
   3. **Schema change** (Ruling 19): if the collection's schema version differs from the generation's, compact (rule 7) with the new layout first.
   4. **Adoption:** if the pointer's manifest version exceeds M_T's, adopt the new manifest (rule 6).
   5. **Fetch:** unless the state is `Overflow`, for each partition with `head[p] < hwm[p]`, `fetch(FetchRequest { stream, partition: p, offset: head[p], max_bytes: fetch_bytes, max_wait: ZERO })`. Apply the records (rule 3), then publish (rule 2).
      - `OffsetOutOfRange` with `requested < log_start_offset` is `Trimmed`: the tail resets (rule 9). If the first fetch after a reset is `Trimmed` again (the live manifest's `applied` is below the log start: a gap the link already skipped, which Ruling 12 and M1.1 P20 make unreachable for implicit streams), the tail sets `head[p] = log_start_offset`, as `LinkApplySource` does (row 0.55).
      - Other errors are logged. The iteration ends, the state stays `Following`, and the next iteration retries.
   6. **Memory:** `bytes` is Σ over the generation's entries of (`source` JSON length + 4 × vector floats + 8 × sparse entries + 96), plus `searcher.space_usage().total()`. The budget adds `bytes` for every tail. When `bytes > max_bytes` or the budget exceeds `total_max_bytes`, the state becomes `Overflow { head }` and fetching stops (§09 §7). Adoption goes on. Once an adoption and compaction bring `bytes < max_bytes / 2` (and the budget allows), the state returns to `Following`, fetching again from `head`.
6. **Adopting manifest M′** (version V′ > V_T):
   1. Collect the chain from M′ back through `parent_manifest` to V_T (exclusive), through `ManifestCache`. A missing link is a *gap*.
   2. **Drop covered entries:** every overlay key whose latest entry has `offset < M′.applied[p]` leaves the overlay (Ruling 1):
      - `latest` loses it (the entries stay in the generation as garbage);
      - its row id leaves `live_now`, its shadow row leaves `shadow_now`, and `shadow_of` loses it;
      - Tantivy `delete_term(_pk)`.
   3. **Update shadows:** for the remaining overlay keys, apply the PK deltas of the chain from oldest to newest (`decode_pk_delta` of each manifest's `pk_delta`; `None` = no key changed). A key in a delta gets `shadow_of[k] = delta value` and `shadow_now` is updated to match.
   4. On a gap, or a delta object that is `NotFound`: re-resolve the shadow rows of every remaining overlay key against M′ (rule 4).
   5. M_T := M′. Publish.

   The folded states of the remaining entries stay correct: `fold(durable_M, ops(A, e]) = fold(durable_M′, ops(A′, e])`, because the link folds the same ops with the same rules.
7. **Compaction** (a new generation). It runs when:
   - garbage entries (covered or superseded) ≥ `compact_garbage_ratio` × entries and entries ≥ `compact_min_entries`, or
   - the schema version changed.

   Under the writer lock:
   1. Create a new generation with the current layout.
   2. Re-append the overlay keys' latest entries in `latest` order, with fresh row ids, re-adding present docs to the new Tantivy index. `shadow_of` and `shadow_now` are unchanged.
   3. Commit, publish, and drop the old generation when its last snapshot is dropped.
8. **`sync(targets, deadline)`:**
   - Return `current()` once `head[p] >= targets[p]` for every partition in `targets`; otherwise `notify()` and wait on each publish.
   - In `Overflow`, when some target exceeds `head`: `Err(Overflow { head })`. Past `deadline`: `Err(Timeout)`. `Failed` → `Err(Log/Collection)` with the failure.
   - `sync` and `current` update `last_read`.
9. **Start, reset and restart:**
   - `start` loads the live manifest (`Local`; none → `CollectionManifest::empty`), sets `head = applied`, and publishes an empty snapshot before the first fetch.
   - A reset (`Trimmed`, or a manifest chain that cannot be walked twice in a row) discards the generation and starts again the same way.
   - Nothing is persisted: after a process restart the first read starts the tail again (§04 §6 rule 3).
10. **`build_range_tail`:** the same apply rules with no follower and no adoption.
    - Start from `durable.manifest()` with `head = applied`, and fetch each partition up to exactly `upper[p]`.
    - The last fetch's records at or beyond `upper[p]` are discarded.
    - Commit once and return the snapshot. `bytes > max_bytes` → `Overflow`. `OffsetOutOfRange` below the log start → `Trimmed`.
11. **Ranged WAL fetches** (carried in: M0 known limitation).
    - `LogReader` gains a moka cache `chunk_batches: (object path, byte_range.start) → Arc<Vec<(u64 /* base offset */, Range<u64> /* bytes within the object */)>>` with 100 000 entries.
    - `read_wal` records a chunk's batch boundaries whenever it reads the whole chunk.
    - When the boundaries of an entry are cached and `gather.offset > entry.base_offset`, it reads only `[start of the batch holding gather.offset, chunk end)`.
    - The tail fetches sequentially from `head`, so after its first fetch every fetch starts at a batch it has seen.

**Tests** (`tests/tail.rs`; fixture: single-node meta, `FaultyStore(InMemory)`, `LogWriter` with a 20 ms flush, `CollectionContext`, a collection with fields `t` Text standard, `tag` Keyword fast, `n` I64 fast, vector `v` dim 3 Cosine, 2 partitions, and a helper `apply_link()` that runs M1.1's link source to completion):
- `the_tail_folds_ops_like_the_link`: 300 random valid ops over 20 keys (every op kind and patch mode, with and without `upsert`). `get` of every key through the tail snapshot (with an empty durable state) equals `verify::fold_stream` (M1.1) of the same records.
- `a_patch_in_the_tail_reads_the_durable_document`: commit `{a:1, o:{x:1}}` through the link, then append `Patch MergeDeep {o:{y:2}}`: the tail has `{a:1, o:{x:1, y:2}}`, and its shadow set holds the durable row id.
- `a_patch_of_a_key_missing_everywhere_is_a_noop`: no entry and no shadow. `a_patch_with_upsert_inserts_when_missing`.
- `an_unchanged_upsert_is_a_new_version_and_an_unchanged_patch_appends_nothing` (M1.1 P32): repeating an upsert appends an entry whose offset is the second write's (the `seq_no` the link will commit); a patch that changes nothing appends no entry, and the key keeps the upsert's offset.
- `the_tail_drops_covered_entries_when_a_manifest_lands`: 50 upserts reach the tail, then `apply_link()`. After adoption: the overlay is empty, `shadow` is empty, the garbage count is 50, and `get` of each key is `Absent`.
- `in_flight_snapshots_survive_a_manifest_adoption`: hold a snapshot, adopt a manifest, compact. The held snapshot still returns every doc and still searches.
- `shadowed_rows_follow_pk_deltas`: key *k* is durable at row r1; the tail holds a patch of *k* at offset o2. The link commits an upsert of *k* at offset o1 < o2, which gives row r2. After adoption the shadow row of *k* is r2, not r1.
- `a_patch_across_a_manifest_boundary_folds_once`: `Upsert {a:1}` (o1), `Patch {b:2}` (o2), then a manifest covering o1 only. The final state is `{a:1, b:2}`, with exactly one overlay entry and shadow = the o1 row.
- `a_missing_pk_delta_reresolves_every_overlay_key`: delete a manifest's `pkdelta` object before adoption; the shadows still equal a fresh resolution.
- `the_tail_restarts_from_the_live_manifest`: stop the tail and start a new one: identical `get` results for every key.
- `the_memory_bound_puts_the_tail_in_overflow`: `max_bytes` = 64 KiB and 1 000 docs → `Overflow`, and `sync` beyond the head returns `Overflow`. `apply_link()` then brings the tail back to `Following`.
- `undecodable_and_invalid_records_are_skipped`: a raw bad record, and a patch setting `n` to `"abc"`: neither changes state, and `head` advances past both.
- `the_tail_indexes_sparse_vectors`: a collection with sparse vector `s`; upsert a doc with `s = {[1, 5], [2.0, 0.0]}`, then patch it to `{[7], [1.0]}` → the tail searcher's `_sparse.s` postings hold term 7 and not 1 or 5 for the live row, `_sparse_w.s` decodes to the patched vector, and `get` returns it (M1.1 `to_tantivy_doc` indexes it, Task 6 searches it).
- `a_schema_change_rebuilds_the_tail_index`: add field `m` Keyword. Docs written afterwards are searchable on `m`, and docs already in the tail are too (Ruling 19).
- `tail_row_ids_are_above_every_lance_row_id`.
- `a_range_tail_stops_exactly_at_its_upper_bound`: records at offsets 0–9, upper 5 → exactly the fold of 0–4.
- `crates/operon-log/tests/fetch_ranges.rs`, `a_fetch_inside_a_known_chunk_reads_only_from_its_batch`:
  1. Use a `RangeCache` with 4 KiB blocks.
  2. Write 16 appends of 4 KiB into one flush, so one WAL chunk holds 16 batches.
  3. Fetch from offset 0, then from the 15th batch's base offset: the second fetch adds at most 2 cache misses (`CacheStats.misses`), and its records equal the tail of the first.

**Commit:** `log: read WAL chunks from the batch that holds the fetch offset`; `query: add the collection tail index`.

---

### Task 4: Read views — consistency resolution, pinned reads and range tails

**Files:** `crates/operon-query/src/{read.rs,tail/range.rs,tail/registry.rs}`, `crates/operon-query/tests/{reads.rs,common/mod.rs}`.

**Consumes:**
- Task 3: `Tail`, `TailSnapshot`, `build_range_tail`, `TailBudget`, `TailState`.
- Task 1: `ReadConsistency`, `RequestHot`, `HotUsed`, `HotTier`, `ServiceError`.
- M1.1: `CollectionSnapshot::{open_version, at, …}`, `CollectionError::ManifestGone`, `live_manifest`, `ConsistencyToken::{offset, normalized}`.
- As built: `MetaStore::collection_head(Linearizable | Local, cid)` (M1.2a): `CollectionHead { collection, pointer, log_start_offsets, high_watermarks, clock_ms }` from one state; it replaces M1.1's `MetaClient::read` closures over `PartitionState::{high_watermark, log_start_offset}`.

**Produces:**

```rust
// read.rs
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct ReadConfig {
    pub consistency_wait: Duration,      // 10 s: how long Strong/AtLeast wait for the tail
    pub snapshot_cache_entries: u64,     // 256 (cid, manifest version) → CollectionSnapshot
    pub pinned_tail_entries: u64,        // 16 (cid, manifest version, token) → range tail
    pub range_tail_max_bytes: usize,     // 1 GiB per range tail
    pub bitmap_cache_bytes: u64,         // 128 MiB of decoded delete bitmaps (row 0.41)
}
/// Decoded split delete bitmaps by bitmap path (immutable objects), weighted by serialized size; one per `Reads` (row 0.41).
/// M1.1's `CollectionSnapshot::deleted_docs` reads the object on every call, bypassing the range cache.
#[derive(Clone, Debug)] pub struct DeleteBitmapCache { /* moka::future::Cache<String, Arc<RoaringBitmap>> */ }
impl DeleteBitmapCache {
    pub fn new(max_bytes: u64) -> Self;
    /// `split.delete_bitmap == None` → an empty bitmap, no cache entry; otherwise `snapshot.deleted_docs(split)` once per path.
    pub async fn deleted_docs(&self, snapshot: &CollectionSnapshot, split: &SplitRef) -> Result<Arc<RoaringBitmap>, ServiceError>;
}
#[derive(Clone)]
pub struct ReadView {
    pub ns: NamespaceId, pub collection: Collection, pub snapshot: CollectionSnapshot, pub tail: Arc<TailSnapshot>,
    pub read_token: ConsistencyToken, pub hot: Arc<dyn HotTier>, pub hot_used: HotUsed,
    pub bitmaps: DeleteBitmapCache,   // every operator takes a split's deleted docs from here, never from `deleted_docs` directly
}
impl ReadView {
    /// Rows of the view: live durable rows of the manifest minus the shadow, plus the tail's live docs.
    pub fn live_rows(&self) -> u64;              // manifest.live_doc_count − |shadow| + tail.live_count()
    pub fn is_shadowed(&self, row_id: u64) -> bool;
}
#[derive(Debug)] pub struct Reads { /* ctx, reader, TailRegistry, caches, configs, budget */ }
impl Reads {
    pub fn new(ctx: CollectionContext, reader: LogReader, tail: TailConfig, read: ReadConfig) -> Self;
    pub async fn view(&self, ns: NamespaceId, collection: &Collection, consistency: &ReadConsistency,
                      hot: &RequestHot, hot_tier: Arc<dyn HotTier>) -> Result<ReadView, ServiceError>;
    pub async fn pin(&self, collection: &Collection) -> Result<(u64 /* manifest version */, ConsistencyToken), ServiceError>;
    pub fn notify(&self, cid: CollectionId);
    pub fn tail_state(&self, cid: CollectionId) -> Option<TailState>;
    pub async fn stop_collection(&self, cid: CollectionId);
    pub async fn shutdown(&self);
}
```

**Semantics:**
1. **Snapshots.** Every snapshot of a loaded manifest comes from M1.1's `CollectionSnapshot::at` (it opens the Lance dataset at `manifest.lance_version`, skipped when 0, and builds the `RowLocator`, with no metastore read). `Reads` caches snapshots by `(cid, manifest version)` (moka, `snapshot_cache_entries`, 5 min time-to-idle), so a manifest's Lance dataset opens once per node.
2. **The tail registry.**
   - `TailRegistry` maps `CollectionId → Arc<Tail>`, and starts a tail on first use.
   - A background sweep every 60 s stops tails whose `last_read` is older than `idle_ttl`.
   - `stop_collection` stops and removes a tail; the service calls it on drop.
   - A tail whose collection record no longer exists stops itself (Task 3 rule 5.2).
3. **`view` per consistency.** Every branch first computes `targets` (partition → offset).
   - **`Strong`:**
     1. One `collection_head(Linearizable, cid)`: the collection record (`None` → `NotFound { kind: "collection" }`) and `hwm[p] = high_watermarks[p]` for every partition of its stream. `targets = hwm`.
     2. `tail.sync(targets, now + consistency_wait)`.
     3. The snapshot is `at(tail snapshot's manifest)`, and `read_token` = `(stream, p, tail.head[p])` for every partition.
   - **`AtLeast(token)`:** `targets[p] = token.offset(stream, p).unwrap_or(0)`. Entries of other streams are ignored (a token may carry several objects, §6.5). Then as `Strong` from step 2; `read_token` as in `Strong`.
   - **`Eventual`:** if a tail is running, its `current()` and that snapshot's manifest; otherwise the live manifest (`Local`) with an empty `TailSnapshot`, and the tail is started in the background. `read_token` = `max(applied[p], head[p])`.
   - **`Pinned { manifest_version: v, token }`:**
     1. The token must name the collection's stream for every partition and no other stream. Otherwise `NotFound { kind: "pin", name: "<collection>@<v>" }`, which also covers a name dropped and re-created with a new stream.
     2. `v == 0`: the durable snapshot is `CollectionManifest::empty`. Valid only while every partition's `log_start_offset` is 0; otherwise `NotFound(pin)`.
     3. `v > 0`: `CollectionSnapshot::open_version(v)`; `ManifestGone(v)` → `NotFound(pin)`. `token[p] < manifest.applied[p]` for some *p* → `InvalidArgument("the pinned token is older than manifest {v}")`.
     4. The tail is `build_range_tail(…, upper = token)`, cached in `pinned_tail_entries` by `(cid, v, token text)`. `Trimmed` → `NotFound(pin)`.
     5. `read_token = token`.
4. **Overflow fallback** (§09 §7, Ruling 1). When `sync` returns `Overflow`:
   1. Take the durable snapshot of the live manifest (`Local`).
   2. Build a range tail over `(applied, targets]` with `range_tail_max_bytes`.
   3. `Overflow` again → `Unavailable("collection {c}: {lag} records are not yet applied; retry after the link catches up")`. `Trimmed` (unreachable for implicit streams, row 0.55) → `Unavailable`.

   Range tails for overflow are not cached: `targets` differ per read.
5. **Error mapping:**
   - `TailError::Timeout` → `ServiceError::Timeout`;
   - `Log` and `Collection` errors that are retryable (`StoreError::is_retryable`, `LogError::{Backpressure, Store, Cache}`, `CollectionError::is_retryable`) → `Unavailable`; other errors → `Internal`;
   - `Stopped` → `NotFound { kind: "collection" }`.
6. **Hot:** `view` records `hot_tier.record_access(ns, cid)` once, and stores `hot_tier` and `hot.used` in the view. The service passes `NoHotTier` when `!hot.enabled` (Task 9).
7. **`pin`:**
   1. One `collection_head(Linearizable, cid)`: the pointer version (0 when absent) and the stream's high watermarks.
   2. Start the tail, so a pinned read soon after finds the range in the log.
   3. Return `(version, token of the high watermarks)`.

**Tests** (`tests/reads.rs`, the Task 3 fixture):
- `a_strong_read_sees_an_acknowledged_write_before_link_apply`: write, then `view(Strong)` without link apply: the doc is in the tail.
- `at_least_waits_for_the_token_offsets`: the tail's follower is paused with `Tail::pause_fetch(true)` → `view(AtLeast(token))` completes only once the hook is released, and within `consistency_wait`.
- `a_strong_read_times_out_when_the_log_cannot_be_read`: the test-only `PathStore` (row 0.59) fails every `get` under the stream's WAL prefix; `consistency_wait` = 300 ms → `Unavailable` or `Timeout` within 1 s.
- `eventual_reads_what_is_in_memory`: without a running tail, the view is durable-only and the tail starts.
- `read_tokens_cover_the_read`: the token's offsets equal the tail head.
- `pinned_reads_ignore_later_writes`: pin, write 10 upserts and 5 deletes, commit through the link twice → every pinned `get`, `count` and search equals its result at pin time.
- `a_pinned_read_of_a_gone_manifest_is_not_found`: `keep_manifests = 1`, retention 0, 3 more commits → `NotFound { kind: "pin" }`.
- `a_pinned_token_of_another_collection_is_not_found`.
- `a_pin_before_the_first_commit_reads_from_offset_zero`: pin at version 0, then commit through the link → the pinned reads still equal the pin-time state.
- `a_pinned_read_after_drop_and_recreate_is_not_found`: pin `docs`, drop it, re-create it → the old pin is `NotFound(pin)`.
- `an_overflowed_tail_falls_back_to_a_range_tail_with_identical_results`: `max_bytes` 64 KiB → reads equal those of a large-bound fixture over the same writes.
- `a_strong_read_through_a_lagging_follower_is_still_strong`: 3-node meta; the reading service's `Arc<dyn MetaStore>` is a follower's client; each of 50 write-then-read iterations sees its write.
- `snapshots_are_cached_per_manifest_version`: two reads at one version → one Lance open (`PathStore`'s `get` count under the collection's `lance/_versions/` prefix grows once).
- `delete_bitmaps_are_read_once_per_node`: two searches over a split with a delete bitmap → `PathStore`'s `get` count of the bitmap path is 1.

`tests/common/mod.rs` (shared by every `operon-query` test) holds `PathStore`: an `object_store::ObjectStore` over `InMemory` that counts calls per path prefix and fails `get`s under a prefix on demand, wrapped as `operon-cache/tests/range_cache.rs:22-53` wraps one (M0's `FaultyStore` counts and fails per `Op`, not per path).

**Commit:** `query: resolve read views per consistency level`.

### Task 5: Text search over splits and tail — `TantivySearchExec`, `FilterBitmapExec` and global statistics

**Files:** `crates/operon-query/src/text/{splits.rs,stats.rs,norms.rs}`, `crates/operon-query/src/exec/{mod.rs,schema.rs,mask.rs,tantivy_search.rs,filter_bitmap.rs,order.rs}`, `crates/operon-query/tests/text.rs`.

**Consumes:**
- Task 2: `QueryCompiler`, `CompiledQuery`, `CompileMode`. Task 4: `ReadView`.
- M1.1: `SplitRef`, `CollectionSnapshot::{splits, open_split, locate_row}`, `open_split(storage, path, split_size, footer_range)` (operon-text, X9, row 0.45), `OperonStorage`, the vendored `warmup` and `Storage` trait. Task 4: `ReadView.bitmaps` (deleted docs, row 0.41).
- `HotTier::split_file`.
- tantivy: `Weight::for_each_pruning`, `Weight::for_each_no_score`, `EnableScoring::{enabled_from_statistics_provider, disabled_from_searcher}`, `Bm25StatisticsProvider`, `FieldNormReader::fieldnorm_id`, `InvertedIndexReader::{doc_freq, read_postings}`, the fast-field column readers (`u64("_rowid")`, `bytes("_pk")`, `i64`/`f64`/`bool`/`date`/`str` columns).
- DataFusion 54.1: `ExecutionPlan` (`properties() -> &Arc<PlanProperties>`, `PlanProperties::new(EquivalenceProperties, Partitioning::UnknownPartitioning(1), EmissionType::Final, Boundedness::Bounded)`), `RecordBatchStreamAdapter`, `DisplayAs`.

**Produces:**

```rust
// exec/schema.rs
pub fn ranked_schema() -> SchemaRef;   // _rowid UInt64, _pk Binary (canonical), _score Float32, _sort Binary (postcard Vec<SortValue>); all non-null
pub fn rowid_schema() -> SchemaRef;    // _rowid UInt64, ascending
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)] pub struct Ranked { pub row_id: u64, pub pk: PrimaryKey, pub score: f32, pub sort: Vec<SortValue> }
pub fn ranked_to_batch(rows: &[Ranked]) -> RecordBatch;  pub fn batch_to_ranked(batch: &RecordBatch) -> Result<Vec<Ranked>, ServiceError>;

// exec/order.rs
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct EffectiveSort { pub keys: Vec<SortKey> /* ends with Pk; see rule 1 */, pub mode: RankMode }
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)] pub enum RankMode { Score, Field }
impl EffectiveSort {
    pub fn of(request: &SearchRequest) -> Result<Self, ServiceError>;
    pub fn compare(&self, a: &Ranked, b: &Ranked) -> Ordering;      // the total order of R10
    pub fn is_after(&self, hit: &Ranked, search_after: &[SortValue]) -> bool;  // prefix semantics, rule 1
    pub fn sort_values(&self, hit: &Ranked) -> Vec<SortValue>;     // one per key, including score and PK
}

// exec/mask.rs
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)] pub enum RowSet { All, Rows(RoaringTreemap) }  // All = every row of the view
pub struct SplitMask { pub deleted: RoaringBitmap, pub shadowed: RoaringBitmap }   // doc ids of one split
impl SplitMask { pub fn masked(&self, doc: u32) -> bool; }

// text/splits.rs
pub struct OpenSplit { pub index_in_manifest: usize, pub split: SplitRef, pub searcher: tantivy::Searcher, pub mask: SplitMask, pub from_hot_file: bool }
pub async fn open_splits(view: &ReadView, warmups: &dyn Fn(&tantivy::schema::Schema) -> Result<WarmupInfo, ServiceError>)
    -> Result<Vec<OpenSplit>, ServiceError>;
#[derive(Clone, Debug)] pub struct LocalSplitStorage { pub path: PathBuf }   // a Quickwit Storage over one local file (hot pinned split)

// text/norms.rs
pub const FIELD_NORMS_TABLE: [u32; 256];     // copied from tantivy-0.26.2/src/fieldnorm/code.rs (MIT), which does not export it
pub fn norm_mid2(id: u8) -> u64;             // twice the midpoint of bucket `id`: T[id] + T[id+1] − 1 for id < 255, else 2·T[255]

// text/stats.rs
#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default)]
pub struct GlobalStats { pub num_docs: u64, pub tokens: BTreeMap<String /* field */, u64>, pub doc_freq: BTreeMap<(String, Vec<u8> /* term value bytes */), u64> }
impl GlobalStats {
    pub async fn compute(view: &ReadView, splits: &[OpenSplit], terms: &BTreeSet<(String, Vec<u8>)>, fields: &BTreeSet<String>,
                         cache: &StatsCache) -> Result<Self, ServiceError>;
    pub fn provider<'a>(&'a self, schema: &'a tantivy::schema::Schema) -> impl Bm25StatisticsProvider + 'a;
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug)] pub struct StatsCache { /* moka: (split ulid, bitmap path, field) → live token sum; (…, field, term) → live df */ }
impl StatsCache { pub fn new(entries: u64 /* 1_000_000 */) -> Self; }

// exec/tantivy_search.rs
#[derive(Debug)] pub struct TantivySearchExec { /* view, query, filter, k, sort, search_after, stats cache */ }
impl TantivySearchExec {
    pub fn new(view: Arc<ReadView>, query: Query, filter: Option<Query>, k: usize, sort: EffectiveSort,
               search_after: Option<Vec<SortValue>>, stats: StatsCache, parallelism: usize) -> Self;
    pub async fn count(&self) -> Result<u64, ServiceError>;              // matches of query ∧ filter over the view
}
impl ExecutionPlan for TantivySearchExec {}                             // output: ranked_schema, ordered by `sort`, at most k rows

// exec/filter_bitmap.rs
#[derive(Debug)] pub struct FilterBitmapExec { /* view, filter */ }
impl FilterBitmapExec {
    pub fn new(view: Arc<ReadView>, filter: Query, parallelism: usize) -> Self;
    pub async fn rows(&self) -> Result<RowSet, ServiceError>;           // durable row ids and tail row ids that match
}
impl ExecutionPlan for FilterBitmapExec {}                              // output: rowid_schema (Rows only; All expands to every row)
```

**Semantics:**
1. **Effective sort** (Ruling 6).
   - Score mode: `sort` empty → `[Score desc]`.
   - Field mode: `sort` empty with no retrievers → `[Pk asc]`.
   - A trailing `Pk asc` is appended unless the last key is `Pk`.
   - `compare` orders key by key:
     - `Score` by `f32::total_cmp`;
     - `Pk` by canonical bytes;
     - `Field` by its value: `Null` first or last per `missing`; `Bool` false < true; numbers numerically (`I64`/`U64` exactly through `i128`, and `F64` against integers through `f64::total_cmp` after converting the integer); `Str` and `Uuid` bytewise.
   - `desc` reverses a key's order, except where `missing` places `Null`.
   - A multi-valued field sorts by its minimum for `asc` and its maximum for `desc` (ES's default mode).
   - `is_after(hit, v)`: compare the first `v.len()` keys of the hit with `v`. The hit is after iff the result is `Greater`. Equal prefixes are *not* after, so ES's `search_after` without a tie-breaker skips equal values, and a full tuple including the PK pages exactly.
   - A `search_after` value at a `Pk` position is read with `SortValue::as_pk`; at a date field position, `I64` is epoch ms.
2. **Opening splits.** For each `SplitRef` of the view's manifest, in manifest order:
   1. If `view.hot.split_file(ns, cid, ulid)` is `Some(path)`, open it with M1.1's `open_split(Arc::new(LocalSplitStorage { path }), path, file length, split.footer_range)` (`get_slice` = a positional read in `spawn_blocking`, `get_all` = the whole file, `file_num_bytes` = file length, `uri` = its `file://` URI; `get_slice_with_file_len` keeps the trait's default, which calls `get_slice`; every other `Storage` method is `StorageErrorKind::Internal("read-only local split")`). Split indexes are only ever opened through `open_split`, which wraps the `StorageDirectory` in `CachingDirectory` and `HotDirectory` (M1.1 Task 2 note), and record `HotKind::Splits` in `view.hot_used`. If opening the local file fails (it was demoted and deleted after the lookup), log it and use step 2 for that split; `Splits` is recorded only when a local file was used (M1.3 B10).
   2. Otherwise use `CollectionSnapshot::open_split` (one ranged GET through the range cache).
   3. Compute the query's `WarmupInfo` against the split's schema and run the vendored `warmup` (its result is always `false`, since `required_terms` stays empty, Task 2 rule 8).
   4. `mask.deleted` = `view.bitmaps.deleted_docs(&view.snapshot, split)` (row 0.41).
   5. `mask.shadowed` = the doc ids of `view.tail.shadow()` rows located in this split: `locate_row(row) → (split index, doc)`.

   Splits open once per request; the range cache and the hot file provide the cross-request caching. Splits are opened and searched concurrently, `parallelism` (8) at a time, with the CPU work in `spawn_blocking`.
3. **Global statistics** (R6, Ruling 2), for the fields and terms the compiled query scores:
   - `num_docs = view.live_rows()`.
   - `tokens(field)`: Σ over splits and the tail of `½ · Σ_{docs d not masked} norm_mid2(fieldnorm_id(d, field))`. The sum is computed in u64 halves and divided once at the end, which makes it order-independent. A split without the field adds 0.
     - Per split, the sum over not-deleted docs is cached in `StatsCache` under `(split ulid, delete-bitmap path or "", field)`.
     - The shadowed docs are subtracted per request.
   - `doc_freq(field, term)`: per split, `|postings(term) ∖ deleted|`, cached under `(ulid, bitmap, field, term bytes)`, minus the shadowed docs whose postings contain the term (checked with `seek` per shadowed doc). For the tail, the postings of live docs only.
   - `provider(schema)` implements `Bm25StatisticsProvider` for one split's schema: `Field` → name through `schema.get_field_name`; `Term` → `(name, term.serialized_value_bytes())`. Terms the query did not declare answer `doc_freq = 0`.
4. **`TantivySearchExec`, score mode**, per split (and the tail, whose "split" is its RAM searcher, with a mask of non-live docs by `_rowid`):
   1. `weight = compiled.query.weight(EnableScoring::enabled_from_statistics_provider(&provider, searcher))`.
   2. `weight.for_each_pruning(f32::MIN, reader, callback)`. The callback drops masked docs by returning the current threshold. Otherwise it pushes `(score, doc)` into a buffer holding every candidate with `score >= s_k`, where `s_k` is the k-th best score so far (ties at the boundary are kept). It returns `s_k.next_down()` once k candidates exist, else `f32::MIN`. Returning the next float below `s_k` keeps docs *equal* to the k-th score in play, so PK tie-breaks stay exact (Review Focus 3).
   3. Read `_pk` and `_rowid` for the buffered candidates, sort by the effective order, and truncate to k.
   4. Across splits and the tail, merge by the effective order and truncate to k.

   A `compiled.constant_score` query has every score equal, so its buffer holds every match; Task 7 rule 11 replaces this path by a walk in PK order.
5. **`TantivySearchExec`, field mode**, per split:
   1. Iterate `weight.for_each_no_score` (or `for_each` when a `Score` key is present).
   2. Skip masked docs.
   3. Read the sort keys' fast-field values (the collection field must be `fast`, else `InvalidArgument("sort field {f} is not a fast field")`; JSON paths → `InvalidArgument("sorting on JSON paths is not supported in M1")`).
   4. Apply `search_after` as a filter.
   5. Keep a bounded heap of the best `k = offset + limit` by the effective order. The `_pk` fast field is read for each doc admitted to the heap.
   6. Merge as in score mode.

   `Hit.score` is the BM25 score when a `Score` key is present, else `0.0`.
6. **`count`:** Σ over splits of `|matches ∖ mask|` (a `for_each_no_score` count), plus the tail's live matches. A `MatchAll` with no filter is `view.live_rows()` without searching.
7. **`FilterBitmapExec`:**
   - Compile with `CompileMode::Filter` and run with scoring disabled.
   - Per split, collect the unmasked doc ids, map them to row ids through the `_rowid` fast field, and add them to a `RoaringTreemap`. Add the tail's matching live row ids.
   - `MatchAll` without clauses returns `RowSet::All`.
   - The operator emits the row ids in ascending order in batches of 8 192.
8. **Operator protocol.** Every operator of Tasks 5–7:
   - produces its rows in its own `execute` from an async block, through `RecordBatchStreamAdapter`, with one output partition;
   - reports `name()` and `DisplayAs` with its parameters;
   - keeps metrics: `ExecutionPlanMetricsSet` with `output_rows`, `elapsed_compute`, and a counter `splits_searched`.
   - `with_new_children` accepts only its own children.

**Tests** (`tests/text.rs`):
- `global_statistics_equal_a_single_split` (the R6 test):
  1. Corpus: 200 docs with field `body` (english analyzer) from a seeded generator over a 60-word vocabulary; 10 queries of 1–4 words.
  2. Fixture A: the 200 docs committed in one link commit (1 split).
  3. Fixture B: the same final docs through 5 link commits of 36 docs, plus 20 docs never applied (tail). 15 docs are also updated twice before their final value, so B's splits have delete bitmaps and B's tail shadows durable rows.
  4. Every query's hits (pk, score) are bit-identical (`f32::to_bits`) between A and B, and in the same order.
- `superseded_versions_do_not_count_in_statistics`: one doc updated 5 times → `doc_freq` of its term is 1 and `num_docs` counts it once.
- `a_split_without_the_field_contributes_no_tokens`: `tokens("m")` equals the sum over the docs of the split that has `m`.
- `bm25_matches_a_reference_implementation`: 30 docs; a hand-written BM25 (`k1 = 1.2`, `b = 0.75`, `idf = ln(1 + (N − df + 0.5)/(df + 0.5))`, fieldnorms from Tantivy's buckets with `avgdl` from `norm_mid2`) agrees to 1e-5 for 5 queries.
- `top_k_equals_exhaustive_scoring`: 2 000 docs in 3 splits plus a tail; for 20 queries and k ∈ {1, 5, 50}, the result equals scoring every doc and sorting by (score desc, pk asc).
- `ties_at_the_kth_score_are_kept_and_ordered_by_pk`: 40 identical docs with keys of all three PK types, k = 10 → exactly the 10 smallest canonical keys (the `U64`s first).
- `deleted_and_shadowed_docs_never_score_or_count`.
- `field_sort_orders_by_value_then_pk_with_missing_last_and_first`; `multi_valued_fields_sort_by_min_ascending_and_max_descending`.
- `a_sort_on_a_non_fast_field_is_invalid`.
- `filter_bitmaps_cover_splits_and_tail`: equal to evaluating the filter on `scan_all` plus the tail by brute force for 30 random filters.
- `the_hot_split_file_gives_identical_results`: a fake `HotTier` returns a local copy of every split → identical hits, and `hot_used = {Splits}`.

**Commit:** `query: add Tantivy search over splits and tail with global BM25 statistics`; `query: add filter bitmaps`.

### Task 6: `AnnExec` and `SparseExec` — dense vector search over Lance, the tail and the hot tier, and exact sparse search over splits and the tail

**Files:** `crates/operon-query/src/{vector.rs,sparse.rs,exec/ann.rs,exec/sparse.rs}`, `crates/operon-query/tests/{ann.rs,sparse.rs}`.

**Consumes:**
- Task 5: `RowSet`, `Ranked`, `ranked_schema`, `EffectiveSort`, `FilterBitmapExec`. Task 4: `ReadView`. Task 1: `AnnParams`, `HotAnn`, `HotKind`.
- M1.1: `vector_column`, `PK_COLUMN`, `VectorSpec`, `VectorIndexRef`, `CollectionSnapshot::{dataset, locate_row}`, `ReadView.bitmaps` (row 0.41); for sparse: `SparseVectorSpec`, `SparseModifier`, `sparse_postings_field`, `sparse_weights_field`, `SPARSE_PRESENT`, `decode_sparse_weights` (M1.1 Task 8, Ruling 27).
- Task 5: `open_splits`, `OpenSplit`, `SplitMask`, `StatsCache` (sparse search reuses the split opening, masks and statistics cache of text search).
- Lance 12 (`dataset/scanner.rs`):
  - `scan()`, `nearest(col, &dyn Array, k)`, `nprobes(n)`, `refine(f)`, `ef(n)`, `distance_metric(DistanceType)`;
  - `with_row_addr_prefilter(RowAddrMask::from_allowed | from_block)` (keyed in `_rowid` space with stable row ids, `:1565-1600`), `prefilter(true)`, `with_row_id()`, `project(&[..])`, `use_index(false)`, `try_into_stream()`;
  - `RowAddrTreeMap: From<RoaringTreemap>` (`lance-select-12.0.0/src/mask.rs:974`).

**Produces:**

```rust
// vector.rs
/// Operon's exact score (Ruling 3): f64 accumulation in index order, rounded to f32 at the end.
pub fn score(distance: Distance, query: &[f32], vector: &[f32]) -> f32;
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct AnnConfig {
    pub brute_force_min_rows: usize,      // 1_000
    pub prefilter_max_selectivity: f64,   // 0.10
    pub postfilter_oversample: f64,       // 1.5
    pub postfilter_retries: u32,          // 1
    pub min_nprobes: u32,                 // 20
    pub nprobes_divisor: u32,             // 16: default nprobes = max(min_nprobes, ceil(num_partitions / 16))
    pub default_refine_factor: u32,       // 20 (ruling 6.1 under "Rulings made during execution"; this block first said 4)
    pub hot_overfetch: usize,             // 2: a HotAnn is asked for k · 2 + 16
    pub hot_retries: u32,                 // 2
    pub scan_batch_rows: usize,           // 8_192 for brute-force scans
}
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)] pub enum AnnStrategy { Exact, BruteForceAllowed, Prefilter, Postfilter, Hot }

// exec/ann.rs
#[derive(Debug)] pub struct AnnExec { /* view, field, query, k, params, allow: Option<Arc<dyn ExecutionPlan>> (a FilterBitmapExec) */ }
impl AnnExec {
    pub fn new(view: Arc<ReadView>, field: String, query: Vec<f32>, k: usize, params: AnnParams,
               allow: Option<Arc<FilterBitmapExec>>, config: AnnConfig) -> Self;
    pub fn strategy(&self) -> Option<AnnStrategy>;     // set once executed (tests and metrics)
}
impl ExecutionPlan for AnnExec {}                     // output: ranked_schema, (score desc, pk asc), at most k rows

// sparse.rs
/// Qdrant's sparse dot product (Ruling 21): shared indices in ascending order, f32 accumulation; None when nothing is shared.
pub fn sparse_score(query: &SparseVector, doc: &SparseVector) -> Option<f32>;
/// Qdrant's `fancy_idf` in f32: ln((n − df + 0.5) / (df + 0.5) + 1).
pub fn idf(n: u64, df: u64) -> f32;
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct SparseStats { pub n: u64, pub df: BTreeMap<u32 /* index */, u64> }
impl SparseStats {
    pub async fn compute(view: &ReadView, splits: &[OpenSplit], field: &str, indices: &[u32], corpus: Option<&RowSet>, cache: &StatsCache) -> Result<Self, ServiceError>;
    pub fn weigh(&self, query: &SparseVector) -> SparseVector;   // each value × idf(n, df[index]); an index absent from df has df 0
}

// exec/sparse.rs
#[derive(Debug)] pub struct SparseExec { /* view, field, query, k, allow: Option<Arc<FilterBitmapExec>>, corpus: Option<Arc<FilterBitmapExec>> */ }
impl SparseExec {
    pub fn new(view: Arc<ReadView>, field: String, query: SparseVector, k: usize,
               allow: Option<Arc<FilterBitmapExec>>, corpus: Option<Arc<FilterBitmapExec>>, stats: StatsCache, parallelism: usize) -> Self;
}
impl ExecutionPlan for SparseExec {}                  // output: ranked_schema, (score desc, pk asc), at most k rows
```

**Semantics:**
1. **Kernel** (`score`), with all arithmetic in f64:
   - `Dot` = `Σ qᵢ·vᵢ`.
   - `Cosine` = `dot / (√Σqᵢ² · √Σvᵢ²)`, and `0.0` when either norm is 0 (M1.4 S9).
   - `Euclid` = `−√Σ(qᵢ−vᵢ)²` (the distance, not its square, as M1.5 assumed).
   - `Manhattan` = `−Σ|qᵢ−vᵢ|`.

   Larger is better on every metric (§6.6).
2. **Validation:**
   - the field must be a `VectorSpec` of the schema (else `InvalidArgument("unknown vector {f}")`);
   - `query.len() == dim` (else `InvalidArgument("vector {f} has {dim} dimensions, the query has {n}")`);
   - the metric is `params.distance` when set (exact only, validated in Task 1), else the spec's.
3. **Candidate rows.** `allow` is the `FilterBitmapExec` of `request.filter ∧ retriever.filter`, or `None`. Split it into its durable part `A_d` (ids < 2^63) and tail part `A_t`. `N_d = view.live_rows() − tail.live_count()`.
4. **Strategy**, first match wins:
   1. **`Exact`:** `params.exact`, or `params.distance.is_some()`, or the spec is `Manhattan`, or the vector has no `VectorIndexRef` in the manifest. The durable part is a Lance scan of `[_pk, _vector_i]` with `with_row_id()`:
      - with `with_row_addr_prefilter(RowAddrMask::from_allowed(A_d ∖ shadow))` when a filter is present;
      - else with `from_block(shadow)`;
      - and `use_index(false)`.

      Every row with a non-null vector is scored and a heap keeps the best k by (score, pk).
   2. **`BruteForceAllowed`:** a filter is present and `|A_d| <= threshold`, where `threshold = max(brute_force_min_rows, full_scan_threshold_kb · 1024 / (4 · dim))` (Qdrant's full-scan rule on the spec's HNSW parameters). The scan is the same as `Exact`, restricted to `A_d ∖ shadow`. An empty `A_d` reads nothing.
   3. **`Hot`:** `view.hot.ann(ns, cid, "_vector_i", manifest.version)` is `Some(h)`. Rule 6.
   4. **`Prefilter`:** a filter is present and `|A_d| / N_d <= prefilter_max_selectivity`. A Lance `nearest("_vector_i", q, k)` with:
      - `with_row_addr_prefilter(from_allowed(A_d ∖ shadow))` and `prefilter(true)`;
      - `nprobes(params.nprobes or default)`, `refine(params.refine_factor or default_refine_factor)` and `ef(params.ef)` when set;
      - `distance_metric` (Cosine → `Cosine`, Dot → `Dot`, Euclid → `L2`);
      - `project(&["_pk", "_vector_i"])` and `with_row_id()`.

      Lance searches the indexed fragments through the index and the rest through its flat branch, so rows newer than the index are found.
   5. **`Postfilter`:** a filter is present and the selectivity is above the bound.
      1. Run `nearest` with `from_block(shadow)` and `k₁ = min(N_d, ceil(k · postfilter_oversample / s))`, where `s = |A_d| / N_d`.
      2. Keep the candidates in `A_d`.
      3. If fewer than k remain and Lance returned `k₁` rows, retry with `k₁ · 4` (`postfilter_retries` times).
      4. If still short, run `Prefilter`.

      With no filter, run `nearest` with `from_block(shadow)` and k.
5. **Rescoring and the tail.**
   - Every durable candidate is rescored with `score(metric, q, vector)` from the projected `_vector_i`. Lance's `_distance` is never returned (R12). Candidates with a null vector are dropped.
   - The tail part: brute force over `tail.live_docs()` that have the vector and are in `A_t` (or all when there is no filter).
   - Merge, then sort by (score desc, pk asc) and truncate to k.
   - Record the strategy.
6. **`Hot` (`HotAnn` h):**
   1. `k₂ = k · hot_overfetch + 16`.
   2. `h.search(q, k₂, allow = A_d when a filter is present, params.ef)`.
   3. Drop every returned row that is not live in the view: `locate_row` fails, the doc is deleted in its split, or the row is in `shadow`.
   4. The uncovered rows (not in `h.covered()`) are searched on the durable path (steps 4.4–4.5 above) with `block = shadow ∪ covered`.
   5. Rescore every candidate exactly, taking vectors with one Lance `take_rows` of `[_pk, _vector_i]` for the hot rows, then merge with the uncovered results and the tail.
   6. If fewer than k live hot candidates remain and h returned `k₂`, retry with `k₂ · 4` up to `hot_retries` times, then fall back to the durable path for the whole search.
   7. `HotError` → log it and use the durable path.
   8. Record `HotKind::Hnsw` when h's results are used.

   With `Operon-Hot: off`, `view.hot` is `NoHotTier`, so `ann` is never called (Task 9).
7. **Sparse search (`SparseExec`, Ruling 21, overview A29).**
   1. **Validation:** the field must be a `SparseVectorSpec` of the schema (else `InvalidArgument("unknown sparse vector {f}")`; a dense vector's name gives `InvalidArgument("{f} is a dense vector")`, and a sparse name in `Retriever::Vector` gives `InvalidArgument("{f} is a sparse vector")`). `idf_corpus` on a `None` field is `InvalidArgument("idf_corpus needs a sparse vector with the idf modifier")`. An empty query returns no rows without opening a split.
   2. **Splits:** `open_splits` (Task 5 rule 2: the hot split file when pinned, recording `HotKind::Splits`; otherwise one ranged GET), warming the postings of `_sparse.<f>` for the query's indices and `SPARSE_PRESENT`, and the `_sparse_w.<f>` column. A split whose Tantivy schema lacks the field (impossible in M1, since sparse vectors are fixed at creation) contributes nothing.
   3. **Statistics** (`Idf` fields only): `SparseStats::compute`. Per split: `n` = |postings(`SPARSE_PRESENT`) ∖ deleted| and `df(i)` = |postings(i) ∖ deleted|, cached in `StatsCache` under `(split ulid, bitmap path or "", "_sparse.<f>", term bytes)`; then minus the shadowed docs of that split whose postings contain the term (checked with `seek`), and plus the tail's live docs. With a `corpus` (`FilterBitmapExec` of `idf_corpus` against the view), each count is taken over the postings intersected with the corpus rows instead, uncached. `weigh` multiplies each query value by `idf(n, df(i))`.
   4. **Candidates**, per split and the tail (whose "split" is its RAM searcher): the union of the postings of the query's indices (a k-way merge of doc ids), skipping masked docs (Task 5 `SplitMask`, or non-live tail docs), and, with a filter, docs whose `_rowid` is not in `allow` (the `FilterBitmapExec` of `request.filter ∧ retriever.filter`).
   5. **Scoring:** `_sparse_w.<f>` is a dictionary-encoded bytes fast field (row 0.62), so per split first collect every candidate's term ordinal (`BytesColumn::ords()`), then resolve the ordinals in ascending order in one pass over the dictionary; for each candidate, `decode_sparse_weights(bytes)` and `sparse_score(weighted query, vector)`; a candidate always shares an index, so the score is `Some`. A heap keeps the best `k` by (score desc, pk asc), reading `_pk` for admitted docs. Across splits and the tail, merge by the same order and truncate to `k`.
   6. `SparseExec` never calls `HotTier::ann`; `Operon-Hot: off` affects it only through `split_file` (Task 9), which returns the same bytes, so results are identical hot or cold.
8. **Defaults:** `nprobes = max(min_nprobes, ceil(num_partitions / nprobes_divisor))`. `num_partitions` is read from the Lance index metadata of the `vec_<i>` segments (`load_indices()`). The largest segment's value is used when segments differ.

**Tests** (`tests/ann.rs`; 3 000 random unit vectors, dim 16, seeded; M1.1 index builds at `index_min_rows = 500`, then 200 more rows unindexed, 50 more in the tail, 100 updated, 40 deleted):
- `exact_search_equals_brute_force_over_live_rows`: for 20 queries and every metric, `exact: true` equals an in-test brute force over `scan_all` ∪ tail with `score` (bit-identical).
- `ann_scores_are_exact`: every IVF hit's score equals `score(metric, q, stored vector)` bit for bit.
- `metric_conventions_follow_the_overview`: hand vectors: Cosine `[1,0]·[0.6,0.8]` = 0.6; Dot `[1,2]·[3,4]` = 11; Euclid `[0,0]→[3,4]` = −5 (not −25); Manhattan = −7.
- `zero_vector_cosine_scores_zero`.
- `rows_newer_than_the_index_are_found`: each of the 200 unindexed rows' own vector finds it first.
- `shadowed_rows_are_never_returned`: the 100 updated rows' old vectors never appear, and their new versions do.
- `a_filter_matching_nothing_returns_nothing`; `a_filter_matching_only_tail_docs_uses_the_tail`.
- `a_selective_filter_uses_brute_force_exactly`: 300 allowed rows → `BruteForceAllowed`, and the result equals the exact search.
- `a_moderate_filter_prefilters`: 10 % → `Prefilter`. `a_broad_filter_postfilters_and_retries`: 60 % with every close neighbour excluded → `Postfilter` with a retry, and still k results.
- `recall_of_the_durable_defaults_is_at_least_ninety_percent`: 50 queries, recall@10 against exact ≥ 0.90 (M1.7 Ruling 8 raises the bar on real data).
- `a_wrong_dimension_is_invalid`.
- `a_distance_override_runs_exact_search_with_that_metric`.
- `hot_results_are_rescored_and_merged_with_uncovered_rows`. A fake `HotAnn` covers the first 2 000 row ids, returns deliberately perturbed scores (+0.01), and also returns 5 rows deleted since its `source_version`. Checks:
  - the deleted rows never come back;
  - every score is exact;
  - an uncovered row's own vector finds it first;
  - `hot_used = {Hnsw}`.
- `hot_off_never_calls_the_hot_tier`: with a counting fake and `RequestHot { enabled: false }`, the call count stays 0 and the results equal `NoHotTier`'s.

Sparse tests (`tests/sparse.rs`; 2 000 docs over 3 splits plus a tail, sparse vector `s` with modifier `Idf` and `t` with `None`, 1–30 entries per doc drawn from 500 indices with Zipf(1.1), 5 % without `s`, 2 % with an empty `s`, some zero weights; 100 updated, 40 deleted):
- `sparse_score_matches_qdrant`: `{[1, 5], [2.0, 0.0]}` · `{[5, 9], [3.0, 1.0]}` = `Some(0.0)` (the zero weight is overlap), `{[1], [1.0]}` · `{[2], [1.0]}` = `None`; `idf(10, 3)` equals `((10 − 3 + 0.5) / (3 + 0.5) + 1).ln()` computed in f32, bit for bit.
- `sparse_search_equals_brute_force_over_live_docs`: for 30 queries on `s` and `t` and k ∈ {1, 10, 100}, the result equals an in-test brute force over `scan_all` ∪ tail (IDF from the same live docs), pks in order and scores bit-identical.
- `idf_statistics_count_live_docs_only`: a doc updated 5 times counts once in `n` and `df`; a deleted doc and a doc with an empty vector count 0; the statistics equal those of a single-split rebuild of the same live docs.
- `docs_without_a_shared_index_are_never_returned`: a query whose indices no doc has returns nothing, even with k = 100.
- `an_empty_query_returns_nothing_and_opens_no_split`.
- `idf_corpus_narrows_the_statistics`: `idf_corpus = Term tag "a"` changes the scores exactly as a brute force over the corpus predicts; a corpus matching nothing gives `n = 0`, `df = 0`; `idf_corpus` on `t` is `InvalidArgument`.
- `filters_apply_before_the_top_k`: a filter matching 10 docs returns only filter matches, and at most 10.
- `shadowed_and_deleted_docs_never_score_or_count`.
- `a_dense_name_in_a_sparse_retriever_is_invalid` and the reverse.
- `pinned_split_files_give_identical_sparse_results`: a fake `HotTier` returning local split copies gives bit-identical hits, `hot_used = {Splits}`, and no `ann` call.

**Commit:** `query: add vector search over Lance, the tail and the hot tier`; `query: add exact sparse vector search`.
