**Produces:**

```rust
// flight_ingest.rs
pub const PUT_COLLECTIONS: &str = "collections";   // descriptor path ["collections", <name or alias>]
pub const PUT_STREAMS: &str = "streams";           // descriptor path ["streams", <stream>] or ["streams", <stream>, <partition>]
pub const ID_COLUMN: &str = "_id";  pub const SOURCE_COLUMN: &str = "_source";
pub const IGNORED_COLUMNS: [&str; 3] = ["_seq_no", "_partition", "_score"];   // read-only columns of a SQL result
pub const ID_TYPE_METADATA: &str = "operon-id-type";                          // "str" (default) | "u64" | "uuid"; string _id columns only
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)] pub enum IdType { #[default] Str, U64, Uuid }
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum PutTarget { Collection { name: String }, Stream { name: String, partition: Option<u32> } }
pub fn put_target_from_path(path: &[String]) -> Result<PutTarget, ServiceError>;                        // rule 1
pub fn put_target_from_ingest(cmd: &CommandStatementIngest, default_ns: &str) -> Result<(String /* ns */, PutTarget), ServiceError>;
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)] pub struct RowError { pub row: usize, pub column: String, pub message: String }
/// Built once from the stream's Arrow schema (rule 3); maps every batch of that schema.
#[derive(Debug)] pub struct CollectionBatchMapper { /* one role per column */ }
impl CollectionBatchMapper {
    pub fn new(arrow: &Schema, schema: &CollectionSchema, id_type: IdType) -> Result<Self, ServiceError>;
    pub fn map(&self, batch: &RecordBatch) -> Result<Vec<DocOp>, RowError>;   // one DocOp::Upsert per row, in row order
}
#[derive(Debug)] pub struct StreamBatchMapper { /* column roles, partition source */ }
impl StreamBatchMapper {
    pub fn new(arrow: &Schema, fixed_partition: Option<u32>, partitions: u32) -> Result<Self, ServiceError>;
    pub fn map(&self, batch: &RecordBatch) -> Result<Vec<(u32, Record)>, RowError>;   // in row order
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct PutAck { pub batch: u64, pub rows: u64, pub token: String /* "v1:…" */,
                    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")] pub offsets: Vec<PartitionOffsets> /* streams only */ }
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct PartitionOffsets { pub partition: u32, pub base_offset: u64, pub last_offset: u64 }
/// The native produce path (rule 7), implemented by the `operon` crate.
#[async_trait::async_trait]
pub trait StreamProducer: Send + Sync + std::fmt::Debug {
    async fn partitions(&self, ns: &str, stream: &str) -> Result<u32, ServiceError>;   // NotFound { kind: "stream" } when absent
    async fn produce(&self, ns: &str, stream: &str, records: Vec<(u32, Vec<Record>)>) -> Result<Vec<AppendAck>, ServiceError>;
}
// flight.rs (changes to Task 12)
pub struct FlightConfig { pub max_duration: Duration /* 10 min, DoGet only */, pub max_message_bytes: usize /* 64 MiB */,
                          pub put_chunk_rows: usize /* 10_000; 1..=MAX_WRITE_OPS */ }
impl FlightConfig { pub fn validate(&self) -> Result<(), String>; }   // Server::start refuses an invalid config (ServerError::Config)
impl OperonFlightSql { pub fn with_streams(self, streams: Arc<dyn StreamProducer>) -> Self; }
pub async fn serve_flight_sql(listener: tokio::net::TcpListener, service: Arc<CollectionService>, streams: Option<Arc<dyn StreamProducer>>,
                              config: FlightConfig, shutdown: CancellationToken) -> Result<(), tonic::transport::Error>;
// sql/json_rows.rs: the per-value half of rows_to_json, shared with rule 3
pub fn value_to_json(array: &dyn Array, row: usize) -> serde_json::Value;
// crates/operon/src/api/streams.rs
pub async fn produce_records(meta: &Arc<dyn MetaStore>, writer: &LogWriter, ns: &str, stream: &str,
                             records: Vec<(u32, Vec<Record>)>) -> Result<Vec<AppendAck>, ApiError>;
#[derive(Clone, Debug)] pub struct NativeStreamProducer { pub meta: Arc<dyn MetaStore>, pub writer: LogWriter }
impl StreamProducer for NativeStreamProducer {}
```

**Semantics:**
1. **Entry points.** The namespace is metadata `operon-namespace` (default `default`), as in Task 12, unless rule 1.2 names one.
   1. **Path descriptor** (`FlightClient::do_put`, `pyarrow.flight`): the first message's descriptor has type PATH. It is answered in `do_put_fallback` when the `Any` type URL is `""`; every other unknown type URL stays `Unimplemented`. `put_target_from_path`: `["collections", c]` → `Collection { name: c }` (name or alias); `["streams", s]` → `Stream { name: s, partition: None }`; `["streams", s, p]` → `partition: Some(p)` (`p` a canonical decimal `u32`); anything else → `InvalidArgument("unknown DoPut path {path:?}: use [\"collections\", name] or [\"streams\", name(, partition)]")`. The response stream carries one `PutResult` per record batch, sent once every chunk of that batch is acknowledged; its `app_metadata` is the UTF-8 `serde_json` of `PutAck`, whose `token` is the merge of every token acknowledged so far in this put (so the last `PutResult` covers the whole put).
   2. **`CommandStatementIngest`** (ADBC `adbc_ingest`, `FlightSqlServiceClient::execute_ingest`), in `do_put_statement_ingest`. `put_target_from_ingest`: the namespace is `catalog` when set (GetCatalogs lists namespaces, Task 12 rule 4), else the metadata. `schema` unset or `collections` → `Collection { name: table }`; `streams` → `Stream { name: table, partition: None }`; any other schema → `InvalidArgument("unknown schema {s}: use collections or streams")`. `temporary` → `InvalidArgument("temporary tables are not supported")`; `transaction_id` set → `InvalidArgument("transactions are not supported")`; `options` are ignored. `table_definition_options` decide what happens, `if_exists` when the target exists and `if_not_exist` when it is missing:

      | Option | Target exists | Target missing |
      |---|---|---|
      | `if_exists` `Unspecified` or `Append` | append | — |
      | `if_exists` `Fail` | `AlreadyExists(table)` | — |
      | `if_exists` `Replace` | `InvalidArgument("replace is not supported: drop the collection through the native API, then ingest with create")` | the same |
      | `if_not_exist` `Create` | — | a collection is created from the Arrow schema (rule 1.4), then loaded; a stream → `InvalidArgument("create streams through the native API")` |
      | `if_not_exist` `Unspecified` or `Fail` | — | `NotFound` |

      So of ADBC's modes, `create` makes a new collection (and answers `AlreadyExists` for an existing one), `append` loads an existing target, `create_append` does either, and `replace` is refused. The result is the number of rows written (`DoPutUpdateResult.record_count`). No token is returned: the provided `do_put` writes only the count into `app_metadata`. Reads are strong by default (R11), so a later read sees the ingest without a token; a client that needs one for `AtLeast` uses a path descriptor.
   3. `do_put_error_callback`: a missing command or descriptor → `InvalidArgument("DoPut needs a flight descriptor in its first message")`.
   4. **A collection created by ingest** (`if_not_exist = Create`): before the first write, `create_collection(ns, table, schema, None)` (Task 9 rule 3: it creates a missing namespace and is retry-safe for an equal schema), where `schema` comes from the Arrow schema: `dynamic: Map` and no fields, so `write`'s dynamic mapping adds a field for every source path of the first chunk with M1.1's `propose_dynamic_fields` kinds (Ruling 18); one `VectorSpec` per `FixedSizeList<Float32, d>` column (named after the column, `dim` *d*, `Cosine`, `VectorIndexSpec::Auto`, `HnswParams::default()`, no quantization) and one `SparseVectorSpec { modifier: None }` per struct column shaped like a sparse vector (rule 3), in column order. The `_id` column is still required. A `List<Float32>` column has no fixed dimension, so it becomes a source array, not a vector. A collection that needs other settings (analyzers, distances, the IDF modifier, strict mapping) is created through the native API first and loaded with `append`. Task 0 checks that `CollectionSchema::validate` accepts a schema with no fields.
2. **Processing** (both entry points):
   1. Resolve the target: a collection with `get_collection(ns, name)` (`NotFound { kind: "collection" }` as in Task 9, unless rule 1.2 creates it by rule 1.4); a stream with `StreamProducer::partitions` (no producer configured → `Unimplemented("stream ingest is not configured")`).
   2. Decode with `FlightRecordBatchStream::new_from_flight_data(stream.map_err(FlightError::Tonic))`. Build the mapper from the schema message, before any write, so a schema error writes nothing. A second schema message fails the put (`InvalidArgument`).
   3. For each record batch, in order: map every row (a row error fails the put and writes nothing of this batch); cut the rows into chunks of `put_chunk_rows`; write the chunks in order, one at a time:
      - collection: `write(ns, name, ops, WriteOptions { atomic: true, report_existence: false })`, the native bulk write path, with its validation, dynamic mapping (Ruling 18) and `partition_of` routing;
      - stream: the chunk's rows grouped by partition, keeping row order within each partition, in one `StreamProducer::produce` call (one `append_many`); the chunk's token is `(stream, partition, last_offset + 1)` per ack (overview §6.5).

      A chunk is written whole or not at all. One chunk is in flight per put, so for each key (collections) and each partition (streams) the write order is the Arrow row order.
   4. A batch with zero rows is acknowledged with `rows: 0` and the token so far. A put with no batches writes nothing: the path form answers an empty `PutResult` stream, the ingest form 0.
3. **Collection columns**, by name, decided once per schema:

   | Column | Arrow types | Maps to |
   |---|---|---|
   | `_id` (required; a null is a row error) | `UInt64`, `UInt32`, or `Int64`/`Int32` ≥ 0 → `U64`; `Utf8`/`LargeUtf8`/`Utf8View` → `Str`, or, with metadata `operon-id-type: u64`, a canonical decimal → `U64` and, with `uuid`, a hyphenated or 32-hex UUID → `Uuid`; `FixedSizeBinary(16)` → `Uuid` | `Document.pk` |
   | `_source` (optional) | `Utf8`/`LargeUtf8`/`Utf8View`/`Binary`/`LargeBinary` holding one JSON object | `Document.source`, verbatim (R4) |
   | the name of a dense vector (`_vector` for the unnamed `""`, as in Task 10 rule 2) | `FixedSizeList<Float32, dim>`, or `List`/`LargeList<Float32>` with `dim` items; a null row has no vector | `Document.vectors[name]` |
   | the name of a sparse vector | `Struct<indices: List<UInt32 \| Int32 \| Int64>, values: List<Float32>>` (the Task 10 rule 2 type, with wider index types accepted within `u32`); a null row has no vector | `Document.sparse_vectors[name]` through `SparseVector::new` |
   | `_seq_no`, `_partition`, `_score` | any | ignored |
   | any other column, with `_source` present | the name of a schema field: ignored (Task 10 rule 2 derives it from `_source`); any other name: refused (`InvalidArgument("column {c}: with a _source column, other columns must be vectors or schema fields")`) | — |
   | any other column, without `_source` | any type `value_to_json` converts | the top-level source key named by the column; a null omits the key |

   - Duplicate column names are refused. A `FixedSizeList<Float32>` column, or a struct shaped like a sparse vector, that names no vector of the collection is refused (`"column {c} looks like a vector, but the collection has no vector {c}"`) instead of being stored as a JSON array.
   - Vector elements must be finite; dimensions and sparse invariants are then checked by `write`'s validation (`check_document`), and `SparseVector::new` errors are row errors.
   - Source values convert with `value_to_json` (Task 10 rule 7), except that a non-finite float is a row error (JSON never carries one, Global Constraints). Timestamps become RFC 3339 strings, which date fields coerce (M1.1 `coerce`).
   - So a `SELECT * FROM kb` result from Flight SQL maps back to the same documents: `_id`, `_source` and the vector columns carry the document, and the other columns are ignored (with `operon-id-type` matching the keys).
4. **Stream columns:**

   | Column | Arrow types | Maps to |
   |---|---|---|
   | `key` | `Binary`/`LargeBinary`/`BinaryView`, or `Utf8`/`LargeUtf8`/`Utf8View` (its UTF-8 bytes); nullable | `Record.key` |
   | `value` | the same | `Record.value` |
   | `headers` | `List<Struct<key: Utf8, value: Binary \| Utf8 (nullable)>>` | `Record.headers`, in order |
   | `timestamp` | `Timestamp(_, _)` of any unit (truncated to milliseconds), or `Int64` milliseconds | `Record.timestamp_ms`; null or absent → −1 (the writer's clock, as in M0 produce) |
   | `partition` | `UInt32` or `Int32`, `0 ≤ p < partitions` | the row's partition |

   The partition comes from the `partition` column, else from the descriptor path, else from the key: `xxh3_64(key) % partitions`, the hash `partition_of` applies to a canonical key (M1.1), so on an implicit stream it is exactly `partition_of`. A row with a null key and no explicit partition goes to partition 0 of a one-partition stream and is a row error otherwise (`"a null key needs a partition column"`). A `partition` column together with a descriptor partition is refused. Any other column is refused. These are the columns of design §02 §7 ("Flight bulk ingest"). An implicit stream (`_collection.*`) is checked by the produce path (Task 11 rule 4): a key that does not decode to a `PrimaryKey` of its partition fails the chunk. Producer ids and stream replay over `DoGet` are M5 (D43).
5. **Limits:**
   - `FlightServiceServer::max_decoding_message_size(max_message_bytes)`, 64 MiB (tonic's 4 MiB default would refuse ordinary batches). A larger message fails with the transport's status before any of it is written.
   - `put_chunk_rows` is 10 000 by default; `FlightConfig::validate` refuses 0 or more than `MAX_WRITE_OPS`. Each document's encoded record is at most 16 MiB (M1.1's writer; `InvalidArgument`).
   - `max_duration` bounds `DoGet` only; a put has no deadline. When the client cancels, the put stops after the chunk in flight, and acknowledged chunks stay written.
6. **Errors:** Task 12 rule 5's mapping. A mapping error is `InvalidArgument("batch {b} row {r} column {c}: {message}")` (0-based). A rejected atomic chunk keeps `write`'s variant, with its `op {i}` rewritten as `batch {b} row {r}`. Once any chunk of the put was acknowledged, the message ends with ` ({n} rows were written before the failure)`. A client resumes from the failed batch: collection upserts are idempotent per key, stream records are not (a resent stream batch may duplicate records, as a retried M0 produce may).
7. **The shared produce path** (`crates/operon/src/api/streams.rs`). The body of the M0 `produce` handler moves into `produce_records`: stream resolution (`namespace_by_name` and `stream_by_name` at `Local`, as M1.2a moved M0's `stream_id`), Task 11 rule 4's check for implicit streams, and one `append_many` for every partition of the call. The HTTP handler calls it with its one partition and answers exactly as before. `NativeStreamProducer::partitions` reads `Stream.partitions` the same way; `produce` calls `produce_records` and maps `ApiError` by status: 400 → `InvalidArgument`, 404 → `NotFound { kind: "stream" }`, 409 → `Unavailable`, 503 → `Unavailable`, others → `Internal`, keeping the message. `Server::start_on` passes `Some(Arc::new(NativeStreamProducer { meta, writer }))` to `serve_flight_sql`.
8. **SQL info** (Task 12 rule 4, extended): `FlightSqlServerBulkIngestion = true`, `FlightSqlServerIngestTransactionsSupported = false`. `FlightSqlServerReadOnly` stays `true`: SQL statements are still read-only, and ingest is not a SQL statement (M1.7's ADBC gate confirms the drivers accept this pair; a change is recorded there).
9. **Scope.** The ADBC Flight SQL drivers (Python `adbc-driver-flightsql`, Go) run against this service in M1.7's exit gate (D49), and M1.6's Python SDK wraps `adbc_ingest`. This plan has no Python in CI, so its tests use arrow-flight's Rust clients.

**Tests:**
- `crates/operon-query/tests/flight_ingest.rs` (no network):
  - `batches_map_to_upserts`: `_id` `UInt64`, `title` `Utf8`, `n` `Int64`, `tags` `List<Utf8>`, `ts` `Timestamp(ms)`, vector `v` `FixedSizeList<Float32, 4>`, sparse `s` → the exact `DocOp::Upsert` list; null cells omit their keys and vectors.
  - `a_source_column_is_the_document`: `_source` plus a vector column; schema-field columns and `_seq_no`/`_partition`/`_score` are ignored; an unknown column is refused.
  - `id_columns_map_by_type`: `UInt64`, `Int64` (a negative value is a row error), `Utf8` as `Str`, `u64` and `uuid` id types (a malformed value is a row error naming row and column), `FixedSizeBinary(16)`; a null id is a row error.
  - `vector_columns_are_checked`: `List<Float32>` accepted; a NaN element, a sparse vector with a repeated index, and a vector-shaped column that names no vector are refused.
  - `a_sql_result_maps_back_to_its_documents`: a batch in `collection_arrow_schema`'s shape maps to documents equal to the originals.
  - `stream_batches_map_to_records`: `Binary` and `Utf8` keys and values, headers, `Timestamp(µs)` and `Int64` timestamps, a `partition` column; an unknown column, a partition out of range, and a column plus a descriptor partition are refused.
  - `stream_rows_without_a_partition_go_by_key_hash`: on a 3-partition stream each row lands on `xxh3_64(key) % 3`, a canonical PK key lands on `partition_of`, and a null key is a row error.
  - `targets_come_from_paths_and_ingest_commands`: every row of the rule 1.2 table, and malformed paths.
  - `an_ingest_created_schema_follows_the_arrow_schema`: `FixedSizeList<Float32, 8>` → a `Cosine` vector of dim 8, a sparse-shaped struct → a `None` sparse vector, `List<Float32>` and scalar columns → no schema entry (`dynamic: Map`).
  - `batches_are_written_in_chunks`: 25 000 rows with `put_chunk_rows` 10 000 → chunks of 10 000, 10 000 and 5 000, in row order.
- `crates/operon/tests/flight_ingest.rs` (an in-process `Server` with `flight_sql = 127.0.0.1:0`; `FlightClient` and `FlightSqlServiceClient` over a tonic channel):
  - `do_put_into_a_collection_is_searchable_at_once`: a collection with a text field, vector `v` and sparse vector `s`; a path-descriptor put of 3 batches (2 500 rows) → 3 `PutAck`s with `rows` 1 000, 1 000, 500 and tokens that parse as `ConsistencyToken`s, each covering the previous one; then, with no token, `POST …/query` finds a row of the last batch and Flight SQL `SELECT count(*) FROM kb` returns 2 500.
  - `the_last_put_token_serves_at_least_reads`: `POST …/documents/get` with the last token in `Operon-Consistency-Token` returns every key.
  - `a_bad_batch_fails_after_earlier_batches_are_acknowledged`: batch 1 has a vector of the wrong dimension → one `PutAck`, then `InvalidArgument` naming batch 1 and the row, ending with "(1000 rows were written before the failure)"; batch 0's documents are readable and batch 1's are not.
  - `a_bad_schema_writes_nothing`: no `_id` column → `InvalidArgument`, and the stream's high watermarks are unchanged.
  - `flight_sql_ingest_appends_to_a_collection`: `execute_ingest` with `if_exists: Append` returns 300 and the rows are searchable; with `catalog` set, it writes into that namespace.
  - `ingest_table_options_follow_the_table`: ADBC's `create` options on a missing collection create it (the vector column becomes a vector; a string column becomes a dynamically mapped, searchable field) and load it, and on the now existing one answer `AlreadyExists`; `create_append` loads both a missing and an existing collection; `append` on a missing one → `NotFound`; `replace`, `temporary` and `transaction_id` → `InvalidArgument`; `create` of a missing stream → `InvalidArgument`.
  - `dynamic_mapping_applies_to_do_put`: a `DynamicMapping::Map` collection; a new column becomes a field and is searchable by term.
  - `do_put_into_a_stream_appends_records`: a 2-partition stream; a put with a `partition` column → `PutAck.offsets` per partition; `GET …/records` returns the records in row order per partition, with keys, values, headers and timestamps; an ingest with `schema: "streams"` and no partition column appends by key hash.
  - `do_put_onto_an_implicit_stream_checks_partitions`: a record whose key belongs to another partition of a `_collection.*` stream → `InvalidArgument`, and nothing of that chunk is appended.
  - `the_http_produce_answers_as_before`: M0's produce tests pass unchanged over `produce_records`.
  - `a_message_over_the_limit_is_refused`: with `max_message_bytes` 1 MiB, a 2 MiB batch fails and writes nothing.
  - `sql_info_declares_bulk_ingestion`.

**Commit:** `query: add Flight DoPut bulk ingest into collections and streams`; `operon: share the produce path with Flight ingest`.

### Task 14: Scan pinning — the scan plan route (D53)

**Files:** `crates/operon-query/src/{scan.rs,service.rs,read.rs,flight.rs,lib.rs}`, `crates/operon-query/tests/{scan.rs,flight.rs,common/mod.rs}`, `crates/operon/src/api/{collections.rs,mod.rs}`, `crates/operon/src/server.rs`, `crates/operon/tests/native_scan.rs`.

**Consumes:**
- Task 9: `CollectionService`, `ServiceConfig`, rule 1.2's collection resolution and rule 9's error mapping. Task 4: `Reads` and its snapshot cache (rule 1), `ReadConfig.consistency_wait`. Task 1: `ServiceError`, `json::token`. Task 12: `StatementTicket`, `encode_ticket`/`decode_ticket`, rule 2 (request metadata). Task 11: `AppState`, the routes table, the token header.
- M1.1: `CollectionManifest::{version, schema_version, created_at_ms, lance_version, applied, live_doc_count, parent_version}`, `CollectionSnapshot::{open_version, at, dataset}`, `live_manifest`, `retained_chain`, `CollectionConfig::{time_travel_retention, keep_manifests}`, `lance_prefix(ns, cid)` (`paths.rs:60`), `PK_COLUMN`, `SOURCE_COLUMN`, `INGEST_PARTITION_COLUMN`, `INGEST_OFFSET_COLUMN`, `vector_column`, `sparse_column`, `PrimaryKey::canonical` (`0x01` + u64 big-endian, `0x02` + 16 bytes, `0x03` + UTF-8; `pk.rs:18-60`).
- As built: `MetaStore::{collection_head, watch_changes}` (M1.2a); the server's bucket URL (`bucket_url(&config)`, `crates/operon/src/server.rs:156-176`: `config.bucket`, or `file://<data_dir>/bucket/`).
- Lance 12.0.0 (verify each at execution): `Dataset::manifest` (`fragments: Arc<Vec<Fragment>>`, `version`), `Dataset::manifest_location()` (`ManifestLocation { path, .. }`), `Fragment { id, files: Vec<DataFile>, deletion_file: Option<DeletionFile>, physical_rows: Option<usize>, .. }` with serde (the JSON form pylance's `FragmentMetadata.from_json` reads), `DataFile { path, file_size_bytes, .. }`, `DeletionFile { file_type: DeletionFileType::{Array, Bitmap}, num_deleted_rows: Option<usize>, .. }`, `lance_table::io::deletion::relative_deletion_file_path` (M1.1 GC already uses it), `FileFragment::count_rows(None)`, `DatasetBuilder::from_uri(..).with_version(..).load()`.

**Produces:**

```rust
// scan.rs — every type Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize; JSON as in rule 2
#[derive(Default)] pub enum ScanAt { #[default] Current, ManifestVersion(u64), Token(ConsistencyToken /* json::token */) }
pub struct ScanRequest { pub at: ScanAt /* (d) Current */ }
pub struct ScanPlan {
    pub namespace: String, pub collection: String, pub collection_id: CollectionId,
    pub manifest_version: u64, pub schema_version: u64,
    pub lance: Option<LanceVersionRef>,            // None while the manifest has no Lance version (lance_version 0)
    pub fragments: Vec<ScanFragment>,              // in the Lance manifest's order
    pub live_rows: u64,                            // Σ fragment live_rows == manifest.live_doc_count
    pub columns: Vec<ScanColumn>,                  // the Lance version's schema, in its order
    pub pk_encoding: String,                       // "operon_canonical_v1"
    pub tail: bool, pub tail_records: u64,         // tail_records = Σ_p (target_p − applied_p), tail = tail_records > 0
    pub offsets: Vec<ScanOffsets>,                 // one per partition, ascending
    pub durable_token: ConsistencyToken,           // (stream, p, applied_p): exactly what the Lance version holds
    pub pin: ScanPin,                              // reads the requested state, tail included (rule 6)
    pub planned_at_ms: u64,                        // the metastore clock of the planning read
    pub expires_at_ms: Option<u64>,                // rule 5; None for manifest version 0
}
pub struct LanceVersionRef { pub uri: Option<String>, pub version: u64, pub manifest_path: String,
                             pub storage_format: String /* "2.1" */, pub stable_row_ids: bool /* true */ }
pub struct ScanFragment { pub id: u64, pub physical_rows: u64, pub deleted_rows: u64, pub live_rows: u64,
                          pub files: Vec<ScanFile>, pub deletion_file: Option<ScanDeletionFile>,
                          pub lance: serde_json::Value /* Lance's own Fragment JSON */ }
pub struct ScanFile { pub path: String /* relative to the dataset root, "data/…" */, pub size_bytes: Option<u64> }
pub struct ScanDeletionFile { pub path: String /* "_deletions/…" */, pub kind: DeletionKind, pub deleted_rows: u64 }
#[derive(Copy)] pub enum DeletionKind { Array, Bitmap }
pub struct ScanColumn { pub name: String, pub data_type: String /* arrow DataType Display */, pub role: ColumnRole,
                        pub vector: Option<String>, pub dim: Option<u32>, pub distance: Option<String>, pub modifier: Option<String> }
#[derive(Copy)] pub enum ColumnRole { Pk, Source, IngestPartition, IngestOffset, Vector, SparseVector }
pub struct ScanOffsets { pub partition: u32, pub applied: u64, pub target: u64 }
pub struct ScanPin { pub manifest_version: u64, pub token: ConsistencyToken }   // = ReadConsistency::Pinned's fields
impl ScanPlan { pub fn pinned(&self) -> ReadConsistency; }
pub const PK_ENCODING: &str = "operon_canonical_v1";
pub const PIN_MANIFEST_METADATA: &str = "operon-pin-manifest";   // Flight SQL (rule 6)
// service.rs
impl CollectionService { pub async fn scan_plan(&self, ns: &str, name_or_alias: &str, at: ScanAt) -> Result<ScanPlan, ServiceError>; }
pub struct ServiceConfig { /* Task 9 */ pub lance_base_url: Option<String> /* None; the server sets it (rule 4) */ }
// read.rs (addition to Task 4)
impl Reads { pub async fn snapshot(&self, ns: NamespaceId, collection: &Collection, manifest_path: Option<String>,
                                   manifest: Arc<CollectionManifest>) -> Result<CollectionSnapshot, ServiceError>; }  // the rule 1 cache
// flight.rs (change to Task 12)
pub struct StatementTicket { pub namespace: String, pub query: String, pub token: Option<String>, pub pinned_manifest: Option<u64> }
```

**Semantics:**
1. **Route.** `POST /v1/namespaces/{ns}/collections/{c}/scan` (alias ok), body `{"at"?: At}`, answers 200 with the `ScanPlan` JSON and the header `Operon-Consistency-Token: <pin.token>`. `At` is `"current"` (the default), `{"manifest_version": u64}` or `{"token": "<v1 token>"}`. `{"tag": …}` → `InvalidArgument("tags arrive in M2 (D52)")`; any other form → `InvalidArgument("unknown scan point {json}")`. Any node serves the route: it reads the metastore, the collection manifest chain and one Lance manifest, never the tail, so it is never forwarded to the owner.
2. **JSON.** Rust field names; `lance` is `null` or an object; `deletion_file` is `null` or `{"path", "kind": "array"|"bitmap", "deleted_rows"}`; `role` is `"pk"|"source"|"ingest_partition"|"ingest_offset"|"vector"|"sparse_vector"`; `vector`, `dim`, `distance` (`"cosine"|"dot"|"euclid"|"manhattan"`) and `modifier` (`"none"|"idf"`) are omitted when `None`; tokens are their text form; `lance.version` is a JSON integer (a detached id has bit 63 set, so it needs u64, as A12 ids do). Example for a fresh collection with two partitions: `{"namespace": "default", "collection": "kb", "collection_id": 7, "manifest_version": 0, "schema_version": 1, "lance": null, "fragments": [], "live_rows": 0, "columns": [], "pk_encoding": "operon_canonical_v1", "tail": false, "tail_records": 0, "offsets": [{"partition": 0, "applied": 0, "target": 0}, {"partition": 1, "applied": 0, "target": 0}], "durable_token": "v1:s9/p0@0,s9/p1@0", "pin": {"manifest_version": 0, "token": "v1:s9/p0@0,s9/p1@0"}, "planned_at_ms": 1750000000000, "expires_at_ms": null}`.
3. **Resolution** (`scan_plan`), after Task 9 rule 1.2's collection resolution (`NotFound { kind: "collection" }`):
   - **`Current`:** one `collection_head(Linearizable, cid)`: the pointer (none → manifest version 0, `CollectionManifest::empty`) and the stream's high watermarks; the manifest through `ManifestCache`. `target_p = hwm_p`.
   - **`ManifestVersion(v)`:** `CollectionSnapshot::open_version(v)` (Linearizable, retention-checked; row 0.40). `ManifestGone(v)` → `NotFound { kind: "pin", name: "<c>@<v>" }`. `target_p = applied_p`, so `tail` is false.
   - **`Token(t)`** (Ruling 24): `target_p = t.offset(stream, p).unwrap_or(0)`; entries of other streams are ignored, as for `AtLeast` (Task 4 rule 3). Arm `watch_changes()`, then read `collection_head(Linearizable, cid)` and the live manifest; when `applied_p >= target_p` for every partition, plan that manifest. Otherwise wait for the next change and read again, until `now + consistency_wait`, then `Timeout`. The target stays the token's offsets, so `tail` is false.
   - `applied_p` is `manifest.applied[p]`, 0 when absent. `planned_at_ms` is the clock of the last `collection_head` (for `ManifestVersion`, `clock_ms(Linearizable)`).
4. **The Lance part.**
   - `lance_version == 0` → `lance: null`, `fragments: []`, `columns: []`, `live_rows: 0`.
   - Otherwise the dataset comes from `Reads::snapshot` (one Lance open per manifest per node, Task 4 rule 1). `version` = `manifest.lance_version`; `manifest_path` = `dataset.manifest_location().path` relative to the dataset root (`_versions/d<id>.manifest`, D34); `uri` = `lance_base_url` without its trailing `/`, then `/`, then `lance_prefix(ns, cid)` without its trailing `/` (for example `s3://bucket/prefix/ns/1/collections/7/lance`), or `None` when `lance_base_url` is `None`. The plan carries no credentials: an M1 reader uses its own object-store credentials (credential vending arrives with auth in M2, D54).
   - Per fragment: `physical_rows` from Lance (when Lance's metadata lacks it, `FileFragment::count_rows(None)` plus the deleted count); `deleted_rows` = the deletion file's `num_deleted_rows` (0 without one; when the count is missing, the difference of physical and `count_rows`); `live_rows = physical_rows − deleted_rows`; `files` = each `DataFile` as `"data/" + path` with its known size; `deletion_file` with `relative_deletion_file_path`; `lance` = `serde_json::to_value(&fragment)`.
   - `live_rows` = Σ fragment `live_rows`. It must equal `manifest.live_doc_count`, else `Internal("scan plan of {c}@{v}: lance holds {x} live rows, the manifest {y}")`.
   - `columns` = the Lance version's Arrow schema, in order: `_pk` (`pk`), `_source` (`source`: `serde_json::to_vec` of the document, UTF-8 JSON), `_ingest_partition`, `_ingest_offset`, each `_vector_<i>` (`vector`, with `CollectionSchema.vectors[i]`'s name, dim and distance) and each `_sparse_<j>` (`sparse_vector`, with its name and modifier). A vector added after this Lance version was written is absent (readers treat it as null). Typed fields are not Lance columns (D36): readers take them from `_source`.
5. **Expiry** (D38, A21). `retention = CollectionConfig.time_travel_retention`:
   - the planned manifest is the live one: `expires_at_ms = planned_at_ms + retention` (retention runs from supersession, which is no earlier than now);
   - it is superseded: find its child (`parent_version == v`) in `retained_chain(…, clock)`; `expires_at_ms = child.created_at_ms + retention`;
   - manifest version 0: `None`.

   This is the earliest moment GC may stop retaining the manifest, measured on the metastore clock that GC uses (P30); `keep_manifests` can only keep it longer. The implicit stream is trimmed only below the oldest retained manifest (D38), so the pin's tail range stays readable until the same deadline. A reader compares `expires_at_ms − planned_at_ms` with its own clock, not `expires_at_ms` itself.
6. **Tail and fallback** (Ruling 22). `offsets[p] = {applied_p, target_p}`; `durable_token = (stream, p, applied_p)`; `pin = {manifest_version, token: (stream, p, target_p)}` for every partition of the stream. `tail` means the Lance version is behind the requested point by `tail_records` records. The pin reads exactly the requested state through the existing pinned reads (Task 4 rule 3 `Pinned`: the manifest plus a range tail up to the pin's token):
   - native API: any read with `"consistency": {"pinned": <pin>}` (`…/sql`, `…/documents/scroll`, `…/query`);
   - Flight SQL (change to Task 12 rule 2): metadata `operon-pin-manifest: <manifest_version>` together with `operon-consistency-token: <pin token>` gives `ReadConsistency::Pinned`; `operon-pin-manifest` without a token, or one that is not a canonical decimal u64, → `InvalidArgument("operon-pin-manifest needs a canonical manifest version and operon-consistency-token")`. `StatementTicket` gains the last field `pinned_manifest: Option<u64>` so `DoGet` reads the same state; `TICKET_VERSION` stays 1 (nothing has shipped between Tasks 12 and 14).
7. **Errors:** Task 9 rule 9's mapping; a Lance error opening the version → `Unavailable` when retryable, else `Internal`.
8. **Server wiring:** `start_on` sets `config.query.lance_base_url = Some(bucket_url(&config)?)` before building `CollectionService`.

**Tests:**
- `crates/operon-query/tests/scan.rs` (the Task 9 fixture; `Fixture::with_local_bucket(dir)` is the same fixture over `LocalFileSystem` in a temporary directory with `lance_base_url = file://<dir>/`):
  - `a_fresh_collection_plans_nothing`: exactly rule 2's example values (with the fixture's ids), and `ManifestVersion(0)` gives the same plan.
  - `the_lance_version_holds_exactly_the_live_documents` (local bucket): a seeded history of 120 ops over 24 keys of mixed types (re-upserts, patches in all three modes, 15 % deletes; the generator lives in `tests/common/mod.rs`, and Task 15 item 2 reuses it), applied in 3 commits. Open the plan with the `lance` crate alone: `DatasetBuilder::from_uri(plan.lance.uri).with_version(plan.lance.version).load()` with Lance's default session and object store registry (no Operon provider). Its `(PrimaryKey::from_canonical(_pk), _source)` rows equal `fold` of the history exactly: no deleted key and one row per live key with its newest source. `count_rows(None) == plan.live_rows == manifest.live_doc_count`; the dataset's fragment ids, data files and deletion files equal the plan's; at least one fragment has a deletion file. Opening the same URI at version 1 (the mainline) gives 0 rows, which is why readers need the plan (D34).
  - `unapplied_writes_set_the_tail_flag`: after a commit, 7 more writes without link apply → the same `manifest_version`, `tail` true, `tail_records` 7, `pin.token` = the high watermarks, `durable_token` = the applied offsets; after `settle`, `tail` false and `durable_token == pin.token`.
  - `the_pin_reads_the_tail_through_flight_sql`: in the previous state, `sql_context_with(ns, plan.pinned())` counts every document including the 7; 5 later writes do not change that count; `StatementTicket` with `pinned_manifest` round-trips and plans the same count.
  - `a_manifest_version_plan_is_that_manifest`: plan `current` at version *v*, commit twice more, then `ManifestVersion(v)` → equal `lance`, `fragments` and `live_rows`, `tail` false, and `expires_at_ms` = version *v* + 1's `created_at_ms` + retention.
  - `a_gone_manifest_is_not_found`: `keep_manifests` 1, retention 0, 3 more commits → `NotFound { kind: "pin", name: "kb@<v>" }`.
  - `expiry_follows_retention`: retention 1 h; a `current` plan has `expires_at_ms == planned_at_ms + 3_600_000`.
  - `a_token_plan_waits_for_the_link`: write, keep the token; `scan_plan(Token)` stays pending while the link does not run; `apply_link()` → it completes with `applied_p >= token_p` for every partition and `tail` false.
  - `a_token_plan_times_out_like_a_strong_read`: `consistency_wait` 300 ms, no link apply → `Timeout` within 1 s.
  - `columns_follow_the_lance_version`: vector `e` (dim 3, cosine) and sparse `s` (`Idf`) → columns `_pk` Binary, `_source` Binary, `_ingest_partition` UInt32, `_ingest_offset` UInt64, `_vector_0` (`vector: "e"`, `dim: 3`, `distance: "cosine"`), `_sparse_0` (`vector: "s"`, `modifier: "idf"`), and no typed-field column; after `add_fields` of vector `f` and no new commit, `f` is absent; after a commit carrying `f`, `_vector_1` appears.
  - `scan_points_parse_and_tags_are_refused`: the three `At` forms round-trip; `{"tag": "x"}` and `{"manifest": 1}` give the exact messages.
- `crates/operon-query/tests/flight.rs`: `pin_metadata_selects_a_pinned_read` (both keys → `Pinned`; the manifest key alone and `"01"` → `InvalidArgument`).
- `crates/operon/tests/native_scan.rs` (in-process server over its default `file://` bucket): `scan_route_speaks_the_documented_json` (create with an alias, write, wait until `link_lag_records == 0`; `POST …/collections/<alias>/scan` → 200 with every key of rule 2, `lance.uri` starting with the server's bucket URL, the token header equal to `pin.token`; `{"at": {"tag": "x"}}` → 400 `invalid_argument`; an unknown collection → 404).

**Commit:** `query: resolve collections into pinned scan plans (D53)`; `operon: serve the scan plan route`.

---

### Task 15: Correctness gates — tail merge against a full rebuild, strong and pinned reads, determinism and the hot hooks

**Files:** `crates/operon-query/tests/{tail_vs_rebuild.rs,strong_reads.rs,pinned.rs,determinism.rs,hot_hooks.rs}`, `crates/operon-query/tests/common/mod.rs`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (the nightly step).

**Consumes:** everything above; M1.1's link source (applied on demand with `operon_worker::run_once`), GC (`GcSource::with_roots` with `CollectionGcRoots`) and trim sources.

**Semantics:**
1. **The query battery** `battery(collection) -> Vec<Probe>`, fixed and seeded. It holds 40 probes:
   - `search`: BM25 `Match`/`MatchPhrase`/`MultiMatch`/`Bool`; filtered text; field sorts with `search_after`; exact vector search in every metric; sparse search on `s` (`Idf`) with and without a filter and with an `idf_corpus`, and on `t` (`None`); RRF and DBSF fusion, including dense + sparse; `track_total_hits` exact; `group_by`; highlight.
   - `get` of every key of the key space, including absent ones.
   - `count` with 6 filters.
   - `scroll` of the whole collection in pages of 7, with and without a filter.
   - `aggregations` over integer fields: `terms`, `stats`, `histogram`, `range`, `cardinality`.

   `run(probe)` returns its full JSON result, with `read_token` removed and every score as `f32::to_bits`.
2. **`tail_merge_equals_full_rebuild`** (proptest, `TAIL_PROP_CASES` cases, default 24; nightly 256). A history is a random sequence of 20–120 valid `DocOp`s over 24 keys of mixed types, from the generator in `tests/common/mod.rs` that Task 14 introduced (extended here as needed):
   - Upserts carry `title` (text from a 40-word vocabulary), `tag` (keyword from 5 values), `n` (an integer 0–9, possibly an array), vector `v` (dim 4, or absent) and sparse vectors `s` (`Idf`) and `t` (`None`) (0–6 entries over 30 indices, zero weights included, possibly empty or absent).
   - Patches also set and delete sparse vectors.
   - Patches cover all three modes, with and without `upsert`, and delete keys and vectors.
   - Deletes, 15 %.

   With 3 partitions, each case builds four fixtures of the same collection:
   - **tail:** every op written and never applied.
   - **durable:** every op written and applied through the link, in 1–4 commits at random cut points (the link is run after each cut). The tail is empty after `settle`.
   - **split:** a random prefix written and applied, the rest written and not applied.
   - **rebuild:** `fold` of the history gives the final documents. A fresh collection receives one `Upsert` per live key, applied in one commit.

   Every probe gives identical results on all four, except `seq_no`/`partition` fields, which differ only in *rebuild*: they are compared across the first three. On a failure the case prints the history and the diff.
3. **`strong_reads_during_link_apply_never_miss_or_duplicate`** (the ES-style write→read cycle, R11). A `Worker` applies the link continuously (batch interval 0), and one writer runs 300 iterations of "write doc *i* (`Upsert`, or every 5th an update of an earlier key), then immediately `search` for a term only doc *i* has, `get` it, and `count`". Every read sees exactly the acknowledged state, with no read token passed. There are never two hits for one key, and counts equal the model. Manifests land during the run (asserted: `manifest_version` ≥ 10 at the end).
4. **`pinned_reads_are_stable_under_writes_commits_and_gc`:**
   1. Pin, then record every probe's result.
   2. Write 200 more ops and apply them in 6 commits, with the tail compacting in between (`compact_min_entries` 10).
   3. Run GC (`keep_manifests` 10, retention 1 h): every probe under `Pinned` still equals the recorded results.
   4. Set `keep_manifests` 1 and retention 0, commit 3 more times and run GC and trim: every pinned probe is `NotFound { kind: "pin" }`.
5. **`results_are_identical_across_placements_and_hot_modes`:** one history in the four fixtures of item 2, each also run with a fake hot tier (item 6) on and off. All exact probes are equal across the eight runs. Mixed PK types, and duplicated documents that force score ties, are part of the history.
6. **`the_hot_hooks_never_change_exact_results`** (`hot_hooks.rs`). `FakeHot`:
   - `split_file` copies every split to a temp directory;
   - `ann` returns a `HotAnn` over the rows at manifest version *m* − 1, with scores perturbed by +0.05;
   - it counts every call.

   Checks:
   - every exact probe equals its `NoHotTier` result;
   - every approximate probe returns exact scores;
   - `hot_used` contains `splits` and `hnsw` with the hot tier on and is empty with `RequestHot { enabled: false }`, when `FakeHot`'s counters do not move.

**Tests:** the five named tests above (one file each; `determinism.rs` holds item 5). `cargo test -p operon-query` runs them with the default case counts; `TAIL_PROP_CASES=256 cargo test -p operon-query --release --test tail_vs_rebuild` runs in the nightly CI job `sim`, as a new step.

**Commit:** `query: prove tail merge equals a full rebuild`; `query: gate strong, pinned and hot-tier reads`; `ci: run the tail-merge property test nightly`.

   - (h) exact sparse search with live-only IDF statistics (Ruling 21, overview R22);

## Rulings made during execution

### Task 0: reconciliation with the as-built M1.1 code

Checked on 2026-09-25 against `m1.1-collection-storage` at `2c6b9c6`. There is one row per Consumes item of Task 0 (rows 0.1–0.52), then rows for the other as-built behaviour this plan relies on and for the M1.1 carries (0.53–0.65). Paths are relative to `crates/`. "Matches" means the name, signature and semantics agree with this plan. Rows that end "(task text changed: …)" were applied to the tasks they name. No capability is missing: `CollectionSnapshot::at` exists (row 0.40), so Task 0's stop rule does not apply.

These rows predate M1.2a. M1.2a (D47) merges before Task 1, so Task 0 runs again for step 3: its rows follow row 0.65, and rows 0.12, 0.13, 0.56 and 0.57 are re-checked against the `MetaStore` trait.

### Tasks 1–16: rulings made while executing

| # | Ruling | Why | Cost if wrong |
|---|---|---|---|
| 6.1 | **`AnnConfig.default_refine_factor` is 20, not 4** (Task 6; `crates/operon-query/src/vector.rs`, the field doc records the measurement). The Task 6 Produces block is corrected | With M1.1's default IVF-PQ compression (`default_sub_vectors(16)`: one 8-bit code per 16 dimensions), refine 4 recalls only 0.72–0.75 of the exact top 10 on the Task 6 corpus (random unit vectors, dim 16, 50 queries), and the brief's own `recall_of_the_durable_defaults_is_at_least_ninety_percent` fails. Measured recall@10: nprobes 20 / refine 4 → 0.72; all 55 partitions / refine 4 → 0.71; 20 / 10 → 0.90; 10 / 20 → 0.90; 20 / 20 → 0.97; 55 / 20 → 0.97; 55 / 50 → 0.998. PQ, not the probe count, is the bottleneck, so the refine factor is the lever that keeps the test and the plan's recall goal | Slightly higher ANN latency (20·k rows re-ranked instead of 4·k). Recall on real embeddings differs; revisit with M1.3's hot tier and index parameters, and M1.7 Ruling 8 measures it. The real lever, M1.1's PQ default, is out of M1.2's scope |
| 10.1 | **Ruling 23's nested calls work as planned; no fallback** (Task 10 rule 5). DataFusion 54.1's `ExprSimplifier` folds an `Immutable` scalar UDF of literal arguments (`make_array` included) into a literal (`ConstEvaluator::can_evaluate`, `datafusion-optimizer-54.1.0/src/simplify_expressions/expr_simplifier.rs:625-647`), so `rrf(vector_search(…), text_search(…))` plans through the `RetrieverDescriptorUdf`s; `retriever_descriptors_fold_to_literals` pins it. `rrf` also accepts a descriptor's JSON text, and a nested call that does not fold is parsed directly, so its own argument error is reported. A third scalar, `rrf`, is registered next to the two descriptors (it returns `{"rrf": [...]}`): the SQL planner resolves every argument of a table function before calling it, so without it `rerank(rrf(…))` fails with "Invalid function 'rrf'" instead of reaching `rerank`'s M3 message | The brief's test `rerank_is_reserved_for_m3` needs `rerank`'s own error, and M3's `rerank` will take `rrf(…)` as an argument anyway | `SELECT rrf(…)` in a projection returns JSON text instead of an unknown-function error |
| 10.2 | **Filter pushdown is limited to what keeps `Inexact` a superset** (Task 10 rule 4). Comparisons (`=`, `IN`, ranges, `BETWEEN`) and `IS [NOT] NULL` convert only on Keyword, I64, F64, Bool, Date and Uuid fields, with a literal of the column's type; Text fields (analyzed: `body = 'refund policy'` would match no token) and Json fields (the column is JSON text) never convert. `NOT` converts only over an operand that is exact and never NULL (`IS [NOT] NULL` and `AND`/`OR`/`NOT` of those), because the complement of a superset is a subset (a multi-valued field matches on any value). A manifest with a split older than the current schema version is scanned without the pushed filter (A4: an old split does not index a field added later, so the filter could miss its documents), and DataFusion's own filter decides | DataFusion re-applies an `Inexact` filter but cannot add rows the scan dropped; each excluded case would drop rows | Fewer pushed-down filters: full scans for Text/Json predicates and after a schema change until the old splits are merged |
| 11.1 | **Get, count and scroll return their view's read token through `*_with_token` methods** (Task 11 rule 1). `CollectionService::{get_with_token, count_with_token, scroll_with_token}` return the result with `ReadView.read_token`; `get`, `count` and `scroll` keep their §6.7 signatures and drop it. A read a remote owner serves carries the request's own token (`forwarded_token`: the `AtLeast` or `Pinned` token, else empty), because `RemoteReads::{get, count, scroll}` return none | The routes answer `read_token` and the token header, and only the view knows the token | M1.3's forwarded get, count and scroll answer a weaker token until `RemoteReads` carries one |
| 11.2 | **Task 11 binds the Flight SQL listener and closes every connection until Task 12 serves it** (rule 5.3). The listener, `Server::flight_sql_addr`, the flags and the startup line are Task 11's; Task 12 replaces the accept loop in `server.rs` (`Flight::start`) with `serve_flight_sql`. Child processes the tests spawn pass `--flight-sql-listen 127.0.0.1:0`, since `operon dev` now binds 127.0.0.1:8082 by default | Task 11's test `the_dev_binary_prints_the_flight_sql_line` needs a bound listener before Task 12 exists | A client connecting between the two PRs sees closed connections |
| 11.3 | **An atomic write's refusal carries `"index"`**: from the `op {i}: ` prefix of the service's validation messages (`rejected_op_index`), from the op parser, or, when the writer still refuses an op the validation passed (a schema change in between), from that op's result, and the request fails although the other ops were written | The service's error type carries no index, and the prefix is written by the same module | A writer refusal after validation reports a failure for a request that partly applied (schema race only) |
